# Skull Paper - Maintenance Guide & Build Plan

This file is the source of truth for keeping the Skull Paper (`/staking/skullpaper.php`)
accurate. It maps each platform feature to its doc page and the code that defines it,
records verified constants, and tracks what still needs to be written.

**When you change a feature in the code, update the mapped `.md` page in the same change.**

---

## Feature → Doc Page → Code Map

| Doc page (`skullpaper/`)            | Feature                | Primary code |
|-------------------------------------|------------------------|--------------|
| overview.md                         | Mission / artists      | Founding & partner artist lists auto-generated from the projects DB via `{{projects:founding:names}}` / `{{projects:partner:names}}` (names only; no X links/logos in the projects table yet). Narrative prose still manual. |
| staking.md                          | Points, store, craft   | db.php (updateBalances, craft/shatter), skulliance.php |
 CLAIMED ITEMS CLUSTER AT THE BOTTOM. getItemsData() orders by featured DESC, project, name -- right for a catalogue, wrong for a shopper, because it scatters the rows you can do nothing about through the ones you can; a claimed card is dimmed with its buy buttons disabled, so each one mid-list is a gap in what you are reading. store.php partitions after the query, not in the SQL, because 'owned' is resolved per viewer and ORDER BY cannot see it. A PARTITION, NOT A SORT -- and the reason is the server, not stability in principle: PHP's sorts are stable from 8.0 and a usort behaves identically there (measured), but ~20 PHP builds are available on this host and nothing pins which serves the page, and on 7.x an unstable sort scatters the catalogue order inside each half. store-order-harness.php evals the real block and asserts both the output and the shape. | staking-membership.md               | Member/Elite/Inner     | skulliance.php:145-212 (role IDs) |
| staking-daily-rewards.md            | Daily streak rewards   | db.php:806-830 (getDailyConsumable, getRewardTiers) |
| staking-points.md                   | All points               | db.php getProjects (founding ids 1-6 / partner ids>7,!=15) - point tables auto-generated via `{{projects:founding}}` / `{{projects:partner}}` tokens in skullpaper.php; no manual edits needed |
| staking-crafting.md *(new)*         | Craft/Shatter/Burn     | db.php:3947-3990 |
| missions.md (Daily reward)          | Daily reward claim     | getRandomReward()/dailyRewardAnnounce() in db.php, ajax/daily-reward.php, ajax/daily-reward-announce.php, dailyReward() in skulliance.js, the strip in missions-daily.php, daily-reward-harness.php. REPORTED: "a bit slow and the button doesn't say it is processing". TWO CAUSES. (1) discordmsg() is a synchronous curl POST with CURLOPT_TIMEOUT 8, and it sat inside getRandomReward() between the press and the reply that redraws the strip -- every claim waited on Discord, and a bad minute there looked like a hang. IT CANNOT BE DEFERRED PAST THE RESPONSE ON THIS SERVER: cryptcrawlFlushPendingSideEffects() in db.php records three production failures (fastcgi_finish_request, then X-Accel-Buffering plus flush), most likely a proxy buffering the whole origin response. The only thing that works is a SEPARATE request the browser makes after it has painted, so the claim now leaves a MARKER in $_SESSION['dr_announce'] and ajax/daily-reward-announce.php posts it. That endpoint takes NO parameters -- dailyRewardAnnounce() rebuilds the text from the database and the session, so a client can only ask for its own claim to be announced, never choose what gets posted. It clears the marker BEFORE the curl (the 8 second window is exactly when a double-fire arrives) and re-writes SessionCookie afterwards, because skulliance.php serialised the session into that cookie several lines earlier and on an ITP/PWA session the cookie IS the session -- the identical trap ajax/dhc-seen-drop.php documents. Markers older than 900s are dropped, or a tab closed mid-claim announces yesterday as though it just happened. The 7th-day dhcf_award() STAYS inside the claim: it is a database write that belongs in the transaction that earns it, and the reply carries the drop. (2) The button did nothing visible until the reply arrived and then vanished, and nothing stopped a second press -- two claims racing both pass getDailyRewardEligibility() before either increments the streak. It now pins its own width (it is an <input>, so it cannot carry the ::after spinner .ms-busy uses on the deploy buttons), says Claiming..., refuses a re-entry, and restores itself on timeout, transport error and non-200 -- that last branch used to be empty, so a 500 left it reading Claiming... forever. HARNESS NOTE: the structural checks read db.php through token_get_all with comments stripped and strings blanked. The first version searched raw source and found the word discordmsg() in the COMMENT explaining why it had been removed, reporting the fix as un-applied; blanking strings then erased $_SESSION['dr_announce'] itself, so the marker checks use a comments-only copy. It also asserts the extracted function body contains `return $project;` and is under 4KB, because brace matching over a 14,000-line file is only as good as the stripping. |
| (payout crons)                      | rewards.php    | rewards.php is now a JOB TABLE plus a runner, not fifteen independent if-blocks. `weekly=true` runs swaps, bosses, gauntlets, cryptcrawl, skullracer, obscura; `monthly=true` runs missions, streaks, raids, factions, monstrocity, guardians, cryptconquest, dhcfighters, dhcarena. Every single-board flag still works and dispatches through the SAME table, so a combined run and a single run cannot diverge. THE HAZARD CONSOLIDATION CREATES: none of these payouts is idempotent -- several tables carry no rewarded flag -- so a combined run that dies halfway must NOT be re-run. Each job is therefore isolated (a throw is reported and stepped over, not fatal to the rest), the runner prints a per-board manifest, and on any failure it prints the exact single-board commands to retry only what failed. `skip=a,b` resumes without double-paying. ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING in two jobs: skullracer pays 'race' then 'lap' then resets (the reset closes the week by flipping reward=1, so it must follow both), and obscura pays then resets. guardians resets itself inside its own function and must NOT get a second reset. run_reward_jobs() unwinds output buffers back to the depth it found, not to zero -- `while (ob_get_level() > 0)` destroys the caller's buffers too, which swallowed the failure manifest. DHC FIGHTERS now has a payout at all: checkDHCFightersLeaderboard($conn, $monthly, $rewards), 100,000 CARBON, windowed on NOW() - INTERVAL 1 MONTH at BOTH ends so it settles the month that closed and is safe to run late but not twice. rewards-harness.php and dhc-rewards-harness.php cover both with no database. |
| missions.md                         | Idle missions, bulk launchers | missions.php (REBUILT), missions-lib.php, missions-field.php, missions-ladder.php, missions-news.php, ajax/mission-data.php, ajax/mission-launch.php, missions-harness.php, db.php (getMissions, completeMission). THE REBUILD: missions.php owns its markup and three partials that BOTH the first paint and the ajax refresh include, so a repainted list cannot drift from a landed one. missions-lib.php returns data where db.php's six mission renderers echoed markup; none of them is touched, so the page reverts in a line. FOUR THINGS THE OLD PAGE DID, worth not reintroducing: (1) it only called getMissions() `if(isset($_SESSION['userData']['project_id']))` and nothing set a default, so a first-time staker saw an empty box under the words "Available Missions" -- missions.php now picks a project server-side and there is no branch that shows nothing; (2) choosing a mission was a full page POST (each card was its own <form action='missions.php#inventory'>), which re-ran verify.php and needed a 9s fake progress bar to cover it -- it is a drawer now and the loader is gone; (3) Maximise and Balance were two more POSTs for arithmetic the browser has every input for -- both are client-side against LO.threshold, which mission_loadout() computes; (4) the load-out lived in $_SESSION across four mutating ajax endpoints and told the staker to hard-refresh when it desynced. SECURITY: ajax/process-mission-nft.php writes whatever nft_id the CLIENT names into the session and startMission() inserted it unchecked (same for items). mission_launch() intersects the request against the eligible set and re-derives the level lock and affordability. Those old endpoints still exist and are still reachable -- they are simply no longer used by this page. NEVER-RUN NOTICE: mission_frontier() lists unlocked rungs with no missions row at all, and the band renders ABOVE the Daily deployment bar because pressing Start All Free is exactly what takes the crew a new rung needs. THE GATE IS HOLDING, NOT CLEARING. It used to skip any project with nothing cleared -- reasoning that level 1 is the start line rather than news, and that a new account would otherwise see forty of them. The fear was right and the rule was too wide: a staker only HOLDS a handful of projects, and for those the free intro is exactly what they want pointing out, since nothing else on the page says they qualify. mission_frontier() now takes a second count -- NFTs held at all, deployed or not -- and skips a project only when nothing is cleared AND nothing is held. Held is deliberately not the idle count beside it: a staker whose whole roster is out on missions still qualifies. The two queries differ only by the NOT IN, so missions-harness.php's stub branches on that; sharing one fixture would test the new rule against the old numbers. Also note $cleared[$pid] is now a legitimate miss, so it is read with isset() -- display_errors is ON here, and an undefined-key notice prints into ajax/mission-data.php's JSON and breaks the parse. The harness asserts no UNSUPPRESSED notice is raised, which means its error handler has to honour the @ operator or mission_art_url()'s deliberate @filemtime() fails it. IDS ARE A CONTRACT: skulliance.js's completeMissions() reveals outcomes in place via mission-row-, mission-result-, mission-reward-, currency-, consumable-; retreat() uses retreat-button-. missions-field.php keeps all of them. The page also REASSIGNS window.loadCurrentMissions after skulliance.js loads, because the bulk launchers and retreat() both call it -- that is what keeps them working without editing a file nine other pages share. CSS trap hit here: `.ms-mcard-stats span` also matched the nested currency span and made it a flex block, breaking "3,600 SKULL" onto two lines -- use `> span`. missions-daily.php replaces renderDailyRewardsSection()'s seven full-width rows (that renderer also RESET A LAPSED STREAK as a side effect -- mission_daily() keeps that write, in the same order); it keeps the five ids dailyReward() writes into (#reward, #claimed, #progress_bar, #remaining, #claimRewardButton), three of which it sets to display:flex, and that path is what reveals the day-7 DHC trait via DHC_SHOW_DROP. PERFORMANCE, all three found on a real account with 179 missions in flight: (a) the page's <style> was at the BOTTOM, so the browser painted every card under flexbox.css alone -- .nft at 25%, art at natural size -- a full FOUC; it is now the first thing after the includes; (b) mission_active() called getMissionConsumables() per row, i.e. 180 queries before first byte -- one grouped query now; (c) 179 cards is ~270KB of markup and 179 images, so mission_active($conn, MS_FIELD_CAP=24) trims IN-FLIGHT ones only (everything ready is always kept) and mission_active_total() feeds a Show-all button that refetches with all=1. mission_levels() memoises getMissionLevels() for the request -- first paint wanted it five times -- and is RESETTABLE, with mission_launch() clearing it; a plain static passed every launch test and silently broke four frontier ones, which is how the staleness hazard was caught. NEW vs NEVER RUN: only cleared+1 is 'New'. A lower unlocked rung with no missions row is 'Never run' -- that happens when a mission is inserted into a ladder below where the player already is. Only the short 'New' goes on the art (84px wide, already carrying the level chip); the long label goes on the action line. ADMIN CAN INSPECT A LOCKED RUNG, NOT LAUNCH IT. mission_is_admin() (user 1) makes locked cards clickable, keeps their real title and art instead of the '??????' + padlock, and lets ajax/mission-data.php?what=loadout return a locked quest -- needed for checking how a ladder is configured. mission_launch() still refuses a locked quest for EVERYONE including the admin, and the drawer disables Launch with 'Locked - inspection only'. This is the split the old page did not make: getMissions() rendered the submit form for locked missions when the discord id matched, the locked card still carried an onclick that pressed it, and startMission() never re-checked -- so one stray click launched it, and a success there jumps getMissionLevels() past every rung underneath. That is the likely origin of the 'unlocked but never run' rows on this account; missions-probe.php tells that case apart from a rung added to the ladder later. The harness asserts the admin refusal and was verified to FAIL when a mission_is_admin() bypass is put back into mission_launch(). CLOSING THE DRAWER STOPS ITS MEDIA. msCloseDrawer() only set drawer.hidden, so an animated mission kept playing -- with sound, on a loop -- for as long as the page stayed open, because a hidden element is still a playing element. It now pauses every video/audio in the body BEFORE emptying it: a detached media element can keep its audio running until it is collected, so removing the markup on its own is not a fix either. Every close path goes through this one function (the x, the backdrop, Escape, and a successful launch). DRAWER CLOSE IS A ROW, NOT AN OVERLAY. It was absolutely positioned top-right of .ms-drawer-box, which is the same corner iOS paints its UNMUTE BADGE in on a muted autoplaying inline video -- so on a phone, reaching for the sound closed the mission. That badge is drawn by the OS over the video and cannot be out-stacked, only avoided, hence .ms-d-bar: a 44px-tall row above the art. Do not move the close control back over the media. QUEST MEDIA AND MARKUP: mp4 quests play in the drawer with `controls autoplay muted loop playsinline preload=metadata` -- controls is what gives the unmute button, and muted is what lets it autoplay at all (no browser autoplays with sound), so the pair is deliberate; .ms-d-art grows to 320px for video because the control bar eats the frame. Quest descriptions carry real markup (links, <br>) and are rendered through mission_rich_text(), an ALLOW-LIST not an un-escape: everything is escaped, then bare br/b/strong/i/em/u are restored (a tag carrying ANY attribute does not match and stays escaped, safe by construction) and anchors are restored only with an href matching ^(https?://|mailto:|/) -- javascript: and data: are dropped keeping their text, and closers are balanced against surviving opens so no stray </a> is left. Two regex traps, both hit: the tag body cannot be [^&]* because the escaped attribute IS ampersands (use a tempered dot), and the href must be captured lazily to its matching closing entity or a query string full of &amp; truncates the URL. The harness covers both plus script/img/onclick refusals. ITEM-ONLY LAUNCHES NEED AN IDLE ROSTER. startMaxMaxiMissions() launches with 'nfts' => array() by design, but renderMaxMaxiMissionsButton() gates on maxMaxiAvailableNfts() > 0 -- the rule is that holding NFTs BACK is what buys the right to spend an item, and once a project's roster is fully deployed you are locked out of it. mission_launch()'s first cut allowed a boost-only load-out with an empty eligible set, which would have let anyone farm missions off item stock forever; it now refuses when $eligible is empty, and the harness asserts both directions. ITEMS vs CREW (drawer): an NFT on a mission is locked for its duration, so crew+boost over 100 is waste. The old processConsumable() enforced it bluntly -- picking item 1 ran clearSuccessRate() (all NFTs + items 1-4) and over-100 raised an alert. The drawer's first cut lost that: it capped the DISPLAYED number and sent the crew anyway. TWO BUGS IN THE FIRST CUT OF THAT, both reported from real use: every item ran the shed, including Fast Forward and Double Rewards which carry boost 0 (so picking Fast Forward deselected NFTs for nothing); and it shed down to `target`, which is the whale-BALANCING share and sits under 100 when a lot of rate is deployed, so a 75% item on a 24% roster drove the budget to zero and cleared a crew that fitted fine at 99. Items now shed against boostBudget() = 100 - boost, never against target, and only when boost > 0. shedByItem records what an item took so removing it returns exactly that rather than a fresh greedy pick; toggling an NFT by hand deletes it from that ledger so a deliberate choice is never undone, and the tool buttons void the ledger entirely. Now toggling an item calls shedToFit() (drop lowest-rated picks until the rest fits target-boost; lowest first frees the most BODIES for the same rate given up) or fillToFit() on removal; a >=100 item also calls clearOtherBoosts(), which spares ids 5/6 since Fast Forward and Double Rewards are not boosts. `target` holds 100 for Maximise / LO.threshold for Balance / 0 for Clear. Tool buttons still do a clean re-pick via applyThreshold. LADDER STATES: unlocked = always openable (you came to read it and see the art; the drawer disables Launch with the reason instead), locked = '??????' title + icons/padlock.png, as before -- showing real titles spoiled every reveal. Art is object-fit:contain everywhere (cover was cropping commissioned pieces); the drawer is 260px tall and contained. BULK LAUNCHER BUSY STATE: do NOT swap the label for "Working...". It shrank the pressed button 155px -> 120px and slid the next one 35px left -- on a phone the row is wrapped, so a button moves under the finger that just pressed it and reads as a misfire. .ms-busy keeps the label in the layout and paints it transparent, with the platform's own btn-spin over it, so the box cannot change size. Measured both ways: old moved 2 of 3 buttons, new moves none. SECTION NAV: the old quick-menu (5 icons, <=700px only, swapped panels by inline style.display) existed because a staker with a hundred missions in flight could not reach anything. It is a sticky .ms-nav now -- every width, nothing hidden, counts on the tabs, IntersectionObserver for the active state (not a scroll listener: this page can be 200 cards tall). The platform navbar is position:relative on desktop so top:0 is free; under 700px the burger is fixed at top RIGHT, hence padding-right:56px. Sections carry scroll-margin-top so a jump does not land under the bar. missions-harness.php covers the writer and the notice with no database. EMPTY-ROSTER NOTICE SPANS THE CREW GRID: the "every NFT you own for this project is out on a mission" paragraph is rendered INTO .ms-d-crew, a repeat(auto-fill, minmax(62px,1fr)) grid, so it became a 62px column of one-and-two-word lines. `.ms-d-crew .ms-d-desc { grid-column: 1 / -1 }`. Button label is Maximize, not Maximise. THE HEADLINE AND THE FIGURES REPAINT: "4 ready to collect" survived collecting all four, and the In-the-field figure read 200 while the nav badge beside it already said 196. Only navCount() and the Ready chip were ever updated; the <h2> and the other three figures were first-paint PHP that nothing touched again. The data was ALREADY THERE -- both ajax/mission-data.php (what=field) and ajax/mission-launch.php return `overview` and every callback was discarding it. paintOverview(ready, ov) now rewrites #ms-title with the same three-way rule the PHP uses (ready / in the field / send your NFTs out), and the figures through data-fig hooks (active, ready, completed, levels); the Ready chip is CREATED as well as removed, since it only renders when something is ready and a countdown finishing under a refresh is exactly that case. With no overview it falls back to reading the active figure off the page. missions-head-harness.js brace-extracts the real functions and drives them against a DOM stub. Two harness traps hit here: a bare strpos for data-fig passes on paintOverview's OWN querySelector, so the markup check is anchored to `<div class="ms-fig..." data-fig=`; and no_comments() lives in realms-harness.php, not this one. STICKY NAV GEOMETRY: the bar pins at top:env(safe-area-inset-top) and paints the strip above it with an ABSOLUTELY POSITIONED ::before that only shows while .stuck. Two passes to get here. Pass one pinned at top:0 and paid the inset as padding -- that covered the strip (body's padding-top scrolls away, leaving it transparent for cards to ride up through into the clock) but cost a PERMANENT inset-tall gap above the bar at rest, ~59px of nothing between the figures and the tabs on an iPhone. Pass two pays it only when stuck. The ::before must stay position:absolute: it then has no layout box, so the bar is the same height stuck or not and the page cannot jump as it sticks (measured 39 in both states, gap above the nav 0px at rest). The .stuck class is toggled in the scroll-spy's sync(), comparing rect.top against parseFloat(getComputedStyle(nav).top) so the inset is never duplicated in JS. A/B'd at a stand-in 59px inset: with the band off a mission card sits in the 0-59 strip, with it on the nav owns it. Realms solves the same problem with a negative top MARGIN instead, which works there only because its nav is the first thing on the page. body carries padding-top:env(inset) and that padding SCROLLS AWAY, so top:env(inset) leaves a transparent band above the bar that cards ride up through into the clock and the battery. A/B'd in Chrome against a stand-in 59px inset: old rule painted a mission card 20px down the screen, new rule paints the bar. scroll-margin-top still counts the inset -- the bar is the inset TALLER rather than the inset lower, same total. THE CONTAINING BLOCK IS WHY THIS PAGE NEVER SHOWED REALMS' OTHER BUG: .ms-nav is inside .main, a flex child with auto height (measured 3753px against a 1323px .container), so the pin holds the whole way. Moving it out of .main reintroduces the one-viewport unpin, and missions-harness.php asserts it stays inside. SWIPE: missions.php loads swipe-nav.js and calls SkullSwipe.init('ms-nav', {blocked: drawer-is-open}); the gesture clicks the nav link, so the hash jump and the scroll-spy's pin both happen exactly as they do for a tap and nothing here knows where the sections are. See the realms row for the refusal list and swipe-nav-harness.js. Three bulk launchers, all rendered from getMissions() and therefore only when at least one mission is already in flight: startAllFreeEligibleMissions(), startAutoMissions(), and startMaxMaxiMissions() (+ renderMaxMaxiMissionsButton(), maxMaxiAvailableNfts(), maxMaxiProjectId(); ajax/start-maxmaxi-missions.php; startMaxMaxiMissionsAjax() in skulliance.js). Start Max Maxi launches 2 missions per unlocked Maxingo level from MAX_MAXI_MIN_LEVEL (=2) up (project DHCF_MAXINGO_PROJECT), highest level first, each with consumable 1 (100% Success) and consumables 5/6 when stocked. Consumable ids: 1=100%, 2=75%, 3=50%, 4=25%, 5=Fast Forward, 6=Double Rewards, 7=Random Reward. IMPORTANT ordering: the 100% item requires the project's NFTs to be undeployed but does NOT deploy them, so Start Max Maxi must run BEFORE either Start All -- the button hides itself once the roster is out, which is what enforces it. Two per level (not three) is deliberate: level durations differ, so pairs come due on separate days and 2 + 1 from the free run equals the DHC 3/day cap. Level 1 is excluded because it claims instantly and would spend the day's slots at launch; maxMaxiUnlockedLevel() gates both the launcher and the button on that floor so they cannot disagree. STEPPING THROUGH THE LADDER FROM INSIDE THE DRAWER: .ms-d-bar carries prev/next arrows (#ms-d-prev/#ms-d-next -> msStep()), and the LOCK DECISION IS SERVER-SIDE. mission_loadout() returns prev_id, next_id, next_locked and next_open, where next_open = next exists && (!locked || mission_is_admin()) -- the SAME question ajax/mission-data.php?what=loadout asks before it will serve a locked rung, so the arrow cannot be live on something the endpoint then refuses. If the client re-derives it from levels the two can disagree and the arrow opens an error; missions-harness.php asserts missions.php never mentions next_locked at all. next_id comes back EVEN WHEN LOCKED so the arrow renders disabled rather than vanishing ("there is more, you have not earned it"). Neighbours are the NEAREST level either side (two LIMIT 1 subselects in a UNION, indexed on project_id+level), not level+-1, because the ladder is ORDER BY level ASC and a project whose numbering skips must still step card to card; the harness's stub INTERPRETS that SQL (reads the WHERE, the ORDER BY direction and the LIMIT) after a stub that re-derived "nearest either side" from the fixture passed with the real ORDER BY flipped. STEPPING IS A REPLACEMENT, NOT AN OPEN: msOpenDrawer() now calls msStopMedia() (factored out of msCloseDrawer) and clears picked/items/shedByItem/target before refilling the body -- otherwise the outgoing mission's video keeps playing detached (the same trap the close path documents) and consumables picked for one mission ride into the next one's launch. Arrows are 44px like Close, and both are disabled while a step is in flight (LO = null) so a double-click cannot skip a rung. |
| missions-consumable-items.md        | 7 consumables          | db.php:2389-2418, consumables table. FAST FORWARD IS NOT A SHORTER DURATION. Nothing stores one: mission_active() (missions-lib.php) and completeMission() (db.php:1393) both pull the mission's created_date BACK by ceil(duration / 2) days and then count the full duration forward from there, so the wait actually left is duration - ceil(duration / 2) = floor(duration / 2) -- ZERO on a one-day mission, which lands the instant it is sent. That is NOT the realms rule: upgradeRealmLocation() (db.php:8730) and the raid launcher (db.php:9708) both use max(1, ceil(duration / 2)), a floor of one day, and they store the shortened timer. Do not copy one into the other. The Skull Paper used to say "round up if duration is a prime number", which is not what any of this does. THE DRAWER PREDICTS IT BEFORE THE ITEM IS SPENT: effectiveDays() in missions.php repeats that arithmetic so Runs for, Net per day and the launch line move the moment Fast Forward is picked -- previously the item changed nothing on screen and the only confirmation was the countdown after launching. Two traps. (1) `items[MS_FAST_FORWARD] === undefined`, never a truthiness test: Fast Forward's BOOST IS 0, so `if (!items[id])` reads a picked item as unpicked -- the same 0-boost trap that made toggleItem() shed crew for it. (2) MS_FAST_FORWARD is emitted from the PHP constant MISSION_ITEM_FAST_FORWARD, not written as 5 again. Net per day is scaled off the server's own net_per_day rather than re-deriving (reward - cost) / duration, and is marked changed only when the figure actually moves (on a one-day mission the wait goes but what a day pays does not). THE ARITHMETIC LIVES IN TWO LANGUAGES, so it is tested twice from ONE table: $FF_TABLE in missions-harness.php is proved by driving the real mission_active() with a Fast Forward item attached, and missions-fastforward-harness.js brace-extracts effectiveDays/daysText/paintRate out of missions.php and reads that same $FF_TABLE out of the PHP harness by regex -- a copied table would agree with itself forever. Verified: changing the server's ceil to floor fails the PHP harness, and every way of writing the client rule wrong (keeping the half instead of saving it, borrowing realms' max(1,..), falling back to the full duration on zero) fails the JS one. |

| games-skull-swap.md *(new)*         | Match-3 score chase     SEPARATE MATCHES OFF ONE SLIDE. checkMatches() used to return one FLAT set of matched tiles and judge a move by allMatches.size, so it had no idea whether that was one line or three: two separate 3-matches came to six tiles and were treated as an ULTRA FIVE, three threes still counted as one five, and an L counted as a five too. Measured before changing anything. It now collects each run and merges runs THAT SHARE A TILE into groups -- so an L or T is one match of five (shares the corner) while two parallel rows stay two matches -- and returns `groups`. forgeFromGroups() is the single place that decides what a slide forges: each match forges by ITS OWN size (4 -> Carbon, 5 -> Diamond), PLUS one Diamond for every match beyond the first. So 2x3 -> 1 Diamond, 3x3 -> 2 Diamonds, 4+3 -> Carbon + Diamond, 5+3 -> 2 Diamonds. multiMatchStep (200 per extra match) is the points half: landing two matches off one swap used to pay nothing beyond the tiles, which actively discouraged the harder move. DETONATION IS ALSO PER MATCH, not per slide: each match that holds a bomb fires one (Diamond preferred), a match of 4 additionally credits the bombs it wasted, and only a match of 5+ fires every bomb in itself. HARNESS TRAP: the stubs hand-copied bonusScores, so adding multiMatchStep made the new bonus score undefined and totals came out NaN -- silently, in a probe. Both harnesses now read the real table out of the constructor with REAL_BONUSES(). BOMB-IN-A-MATCH RULE, settled over several rounds and worth stating exactly because I got it wrong twice. THE ORIGINAL SINGLE DETONATION FIRES AT EVERY SIZE and everything else is ADDITIVE to it: the bomb the match picked (Diamond over Carbon) goes off first, before any chain it sets off -- verified by ordering, matched bomb then chained stray then forged bomb. On top of that: a FOUR credits every bomb EXCEPT the one that fired, at that bomb's own detonation bonus (Carbon 50 / Diamond 100), and forges the Carbon it earned, which fires after. A FIVE is the ultra match -- every bomb in the line detonates, plus the Diamond it forges, plus bombComboStep per bomb beyond the first. A THREE gets the single detonation and nothing else: no waste credit, by decision. CONSEQUENCE WORTH KNOWING: in a 3-match, one carbon and three carbons score identically (235), because only one fires and the others are not paid for -- skullswap-harness.js asserts that equality deliberately, where it used to assert 'each extra carbon is worth more'. 4befb56d had made EVERY bomb in ANY match detonate; the symptom it chased was real (four Diamonds scoring less than three) and the fix was to change a rule nobody asked to change. That inversion is now covered directly: the harness asserts four Diamonds outscore three. 4befb56d had made every bomb in ANY match detonate on the argument that four Diamonds for 40 points was the worst move in the game; right about the symptom, wrong to fix it by changing the rule. The size gate lives in resolveMatches() (>= 4 reaches handleBombMatches at all) and isUltra inside it (>= 5 detonates). BOTH halves need a test: routing a 3-match into the bomb path fires nothing anyway, so counting detonations alone passes a three that is quietly collecting the four's credit -- skullswap-bombmatch-harness.js asserts the 3-match scores exactly 30. It drives the real Match3Game through resolveMatches (not straight to handleBombMatches) so the router is covered, and node 26 needs Object.defineProperty for globalThis.navigator, which the older skullswap harnesses predate. | skullswap.php, db.php:5019-5136  MEMBERS SKIP THE LANDING: skullswap.php is one file holding both #ss-landing and #game-container, and the landing rendered for EVERYONE with ssPlay() hiding it on click -- so the Play menu, which only exists for a member, served the pitch on every attempt to play. That is the same annoyance 204f87e7 removed for the other five games; Skull Swap was missed because it has no separate *game.php. $ss_straight_to_game sets the two initial display values server-side (not by calling ssPlay() on load, which would flash the landing first) and hides #ss-exit, the landing-only pill -- GO BACK under the board is a member's way out. The board is safe to start visible: `const game = new Match3Game()` runs unconditionally at script end and ssPlay() only ever reveals an already-initialised game. Audited all 14 Play targets; Skull Swap was the only one with this gate.  BOMB-ON-BOMB MATCHES (skullswap-harness.js, node only -- the scoring lives entirely in the browser so no PHP harness can reach it). handleBombMatches() used to clear every matched tile and then detonate exactly ONE of them, and it computed the match-size bomb and threw it away because only the no-bomb path uses it -- so lining up four Diamonds deleted three for 10 points each and was the worst-scoring move in the game. Now every bomb in the match detonates in turn via handleBombDetonation() (which refills between, which is what makes the second and third worth anything), the match forges its size bomb and detonates that too, and bonusScores.bombComboStep pays 150 per bomb beyond the first -- deliberately below one diamond detonation (~780), because the reward should be the blasts. Measured: 1 diamond 1,025 / 4 diamonds 3,845 / 5 diamonds 5,350. THE CEILING MOVED WITH IT: SWAP_MAX_SCORE was 25,000, derived from '25 matches x ~790' which only held while one match could fire one bomb. An ultra costs ~6 of the 25 matches to set up, so 4 is the practical maximum and a perfect game is ~22,400; the cap is 30,000. A cap below a legitimate run does not catch a cheat, it makes save-swap-score.php answer 'error' and the record is gone. The harness asserts the cap stays above a perfect game and below 3x it. STUB TRAPS, both hit: tiles need a truthy .element or clearBoard()/clearRowAndColumn() count nothing and every scenario scores the same; and showerTiles() must really refill. THE LAST MOVE (skullswap-endgame-harness.js). Two bugs, one cause, both reported from a real round: a bomb earned on match 25 never blasted, and the score shown never matched the one saved. resolveMatches() was SYNCHRONOUS -- it returned true the instant it dispatched, while handleMatches()/handleBombMatches() were still running behind a 300ms setTimeout -- and the swap handler did not await it, so matchCount hit 25 and endGame() set isGrandFinale FIRST. Both handlers refuse to forge a bomb while that flag is up (`if (bombType && !this.isGrandFinale)`, `if (forged && !this.isGrandFinale)`), so the final match silently lost its bomb and the grand finale only caught bombs already on the board. Same cause for the score: endGame() ends with saveSwapScore(this.score), and its own `await this.handleMatches(...)` was awaiting UNDEFINED because handleMatches was not async -- so the save read a score the pending handler had not finished adding to. FIX: handleMatches and resolveMatches are `async`, the 300ms is `await new Promise(...)`, and BOTH call sites await -- the swap site (~1693) and the cascade site (~2051). THE CASCADE SITE IS NOT OPTIONAL: an un-awaited async call returns a Promise, which is always truthy, so `if (hasMatches)` would never be false and matchCheckCount would never reset. The harness drives the real class through move 25 both ways and prints the difference (not awaited: no bomb, 80 points; awaited: carbon, 280 shown and 280 saved), and asserts both methods still return Promises -- which is the exact property endGame() depends on. |
| games-gauntlets.md *(new)*          | NFT roguelike          | gauntlets.php, db.php (gauntlet* functions, ~12932-13300), plus gauntlets-harness.php. **THE HAND IS SPREAD ACROSS COLLECTIONS, NOT DRAWN FLAT.** gauntletStartRun() used one `ORDER BY RAND() LIMIT 6` over every NFT the player owns, so a holder of 200 of one collection drew a hand of almost nothing else -- reported from a real run: five of six cards from one collection. IT IS NOT A COSMETIC PROBLEM: gauntletGetEffectiveProjectId() maps core projects 1-6 to THEMSELVES, so every card from one core-project collection shares an effective project and gauntletCalculateWinChance() returns identical odds for all of them. Five same-collection cards is ONE choice wearing five costumes, and it makes Fast Forward -- which exists to let you swap AFTER seeing the opponent -- buy nothing. (Partner projects 8+ get rand(1,6) PER NFT so they do vary; Diamond Skull 15 is always 0/50%. The bug bites hardest on core collections.) gauntletPlanHand() decides how many to take per collection and is PURE -- no DB, no randomness, caller shuffles -- which is the only reason it is testable; gauntletPickHandNFTs() does the two query shapes around it. Collections are dealt ONE PER PROJECT before any project gets a second, because project is what sets the odds while collection is what the player sees. It PREFERS variety, it does not demand it: one collection still yields a full hand, which is the correct answer when there is nothing to vary. TWO QUERY SHAPES, NOT ONE: a GROUP BY to learn the inventory, then one small `ORDER BY RAND() LIMIT k` per planned collection (at most 6). Pulling every row to shuffle in PHP was the alternative and the whole point is players holding hundreds. `$exclude` (already-played NFTs, gauntletGetUsedNFTIds) must be applied to BOTH queries. The final `shuffle($out)` is load-bearing: gauntletGetHand() renders in insertion order, so without it the hand is laid out collection by collection. HARNESS NOTE WORTH KEEPING: the loop terminators are asserted AT THE TOP of gauntlets-harness.php, before any planner call, and the harness exit(1)s if one is missing. Several cases deliberately request more cards than the inventory holds -- exactly what the no-progress break ends -- so with that break deleted the harness does not fail, it HANGS. It hung twice during mutation testing, the second time after the check had been moved earlier but was still below the first unsatisfiable case. Also: the `$size < 1 || !$inventory` early return is a FAST PATH, not a guard (mutation proved behaviour is identical without it), and the intval() on `available` only diverges for a NON-numeric value, which is what the harness uses to pin it. |

| games-dhc-fighters.md (trait status) | The mobile shortcut to "Where traits drop" | dhc-nav.php ($dhcnav_items, the 5th field), dhcfighters.php (`id="drops"`). "What can I still pull today" is the single most-repeated question a trait hunter has -- several times a day -- and on a phone dhcfighters.php stacks, so the list sits below the assembler, the canvas, the draw order and twenty roster cards. IT IS AN ANCHOR, NOT A PAGE. A dedicated dhcstatus.php was built and thrown away: a second URL, a second menu entry in Play and a second copy of the panel's markup, all to show a list that is already rendered, is a worse trade than a hash. $dhcnav_items rows are now **[href, label, hint, gated, mobile_only]** -- a 4-field row is an undefined offset on EVERY DHC page at once, not just the one that added it, so the harness counts the fields. Hidden on desktop with `.dhcnav a.only-mobile{display:none}` rather than skipped in PHP: one menu, one markup, no server-side device guess. Under 700px the strip WRAPS to two rows instead of scrolling sideways -- five chips do not fit a phone and a horizontal scroller hides the overflow behind a gesture nobody is told about, the same trap the trait grid had. It is in normal flow below the navbar, so it cannot reach the burger. Label is the noun and hint is the verb, matching Fighters/build, Collection/browse, Arena/fight: hence **Games/review**. Measured at 360/400: five chips, two rows, 83px, nothing clipped, no sideways scroll; at 1000: four chips, one row. Guests get it as a locked "sign in" chip, same as Fighters. **A FRAGMENT AIMED AT THE CURRENT PAGE IS COLLAPSED TO JUST THE HASH**, and that is not cosmetic: "page.php#frag" only jumps in place when everything before the "#" matches the current URL EXACTLY -- it does not while editing (?edit=12) -- and any mismatch makes it a NAVIGATION that reloads the assembler, the trait index, the roster art and two leaderboards to reach a list already further down the same document. In the installed PWA that reload was reported as never finishing. The collapse is derived from $dhcnav_items[$dhcnav_at][0], not special-cased on 'status', so a future fragment item needs no new code. (The service worker is a deliberate no-op, so it was never caching anything -- the reload really was a reload.) THE INTRO FOLDS ON A PHONE, `<details class="dhcf-note" open>` with the summary hidden on desktop, so a desktop renders exactly the paragraph it replaced (74px) and a phone opens at 20px instead of 245px. **`open` is in the MARKUP and removed by script**, so with JavaScript off a phone gets the full text rather than a notice it cannot reach -- and it is a fold, not a deletion, because the second half is the RIGHTS NOTICE (not an NFT, no ownership of the artwork, Maxingo's art). The harness asserts those sentences still exist. A matchMedia listener handles rotation, and a `toggle` listener stops a resize overriding a choice the reader made. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (editing)    | What clicking a saved Fighter does | dhcfighters.php (.dhcf-card .art, $dhcf_edit, EDITING). **CLICKING A FIGHTER EDITS IT. There is no Edit link any more.** It used to only LOAD the build onto the canvas, which looks identical to editing and is not: the traits stay committed to that Fighter, so the picker greys them out and Save refuses -- and you then had to find a separate Edit link to do the thing you had already asked for. Worse, the two states could DISAGREE: editing A and then clicking B swapped the canvas to B's build while the page was still editing A, so Save would have turned A into a copy of B and then failed on the duplicate trait set, reporting something the player never asked to do. One meaning per click fixes both at once. It is a NAVIGATION (`?edit=`), not a canvas swap, because only the server can free that Fighter's own traits back to it -- dhcf_available()'s $ignore_id -- and that is the entire difference between looking at a build and being able to save one. Clicking the Fighter already open is a no-op, or the reload would discard the canvas. A fresh load starts above the header, the stats strip and the save bar, so `if (EDITING)` scrolls .shell into view on arrival; the old in-page scroll went with the old handler and the complaint it fixed would otherwise have come straight back. THE "WHY ARE THESE GREY" BANNER REPORTS A COUNT, NOT ONE TILE'S REASON. It used to quote the FIRST blocked tile verbatim, so one committed torso among forty free ones announced "Your only copy is in a saved Fighter" on a fresh page with a random build -- read as a claim about the trait you are wearing. Rule now: ALL blocked -> quote the reason (true of the whole tab, and usually a conflict rather than ownership); SOME blocked -> a count with NO cause, because blockedReason() returns ownership, head/headgear conflicts AND arms/weapon exclusivity, so naming one would be wrong on the Headgear tab. Per-tile reasons stay in each greyed tile's tooltip. THE SAVE NOTE AND THE SAVE RESULT SHARE ONE LINE, and they used to arbitrate by SNIFFING style.opacity -- syncSave only cleared its own note if the opacity still read ".6". That is not state, it is a guess about who wrote last, and it is wrong the moment anything else touches the element: the note then sits under an ENABLED Save button insisting a background is missing while one is on the canvas. A $sayIsNote flag owns it now, released by msg(). syncSave also re-runs on keyup/change/dragend and once on load, not just click -- a click covers picking a trait but not a build arriving from ?build=, an edit preload, a keyboard pick or the layer drag, and the first wrong state used to stick until something else was clicked. LEAVING EDIT MODE IS A HOLLOW BUTTON, not a quiet link: edit is a state the whole page is in, and the way out of it was an 11px grey word beside a filled button. Same box as Update Fighter (both 35px, top-aligned) -- filled is what you came to do, outlined is the way back -- rather than a second filled button competing with it. The card being edited is marked in the roster (border + an EDITING strip) because the save bar that names it is at the top of the page and the roster is beside the picker -- on a tall page they are nowhere near each other. LOST DELIBERATELY: loading a build you cannot save, as a starting point for a different one. It was never really usable (every piece is committed, so you had to swap enough of them to free a saveable set) and it was the source of the confusion. |

Measured: Arial/Arial/Arial for body, title and close button before; JetBrains Mono / Archivo Black / JetBrains Mono after, with non-DHC content still Arial. **AND dhc-assembler.php DECLARED h1,h2,h3 UNSCOPED**, restyling the host page document-wide -- which is why the panel looked right on the assembler and wrong on the Collection: a rule leaking was propping up a rule missing. dhc-nav.php declares nothing and travels across all four pages, so it is in the list too. Form controls are named explicitly (they do not inherit font). NOT LOADED, EITHER FAMILY: no @font-face and no Google Fonts link anywhere, so JetBrains Mono resolves to ui-monospace/Menlo and Archivo Black to Impact -- the fix is about reaching every element with the same STACK, not about shipping fonts. SIZES LEFT ALONE: the Arena runs 13px to everyone else is 14px and its board is laid out around that. The partial is INERT ON INCLUDE (a function, not output) because every DHC page requires its libraries before header.php has emitted anything. | games-dhc-fighters.md (the credit) | Crediting the artist | dhcfighters-config.php (DHCF_ARTIST, DHCF_ARTIST_X, dhcf_artist_link()). Every DHC intro links Maxingo to x.com/MMAXI404 -- the Collection, the assembler, the sandbox, the marketing page and the Skull Paper pages. ONE DEFINITION: a credit that is a link on one page and plain text on the next is a credit nobody follows, and dhcgame.php had its own hardcoded copy of the URL before this. DHCF_POLICY / DHCF_COLLECTION_URL / dhcf_collection_link() do the same for the MINTED COLLECTION: the rights notice ends "the genuine, ownable Fighters are from the official NFT collection", which without a link names a destination and withholds it. dhcgame.php read its own hardcoded copy of that URL too and now reads the shared one. **homepage.php LINKS A DIFFERENT HANDLE** (x.com/madmaxi__) in its partner row; left alone deliberately, since which account is current for a partner listing is not something the DHC config should guess at -- raised with the owner instead. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (the panel)  | The Fighter detail panel, shared by two pages | dhc-fighter-modal.php, ajax/dhc-fighter.php, plus dhc-share-harness.js. THE BUILDER LINK NAMES A COUNT -- "see their 27 Fighters" is worth a click where "see their Fighters" is not. Its own grouped query in dhcgallery.php and its own COUNT in ajax/dhc-fighter.php, NOT a tally of the rows on screen: those are whatever the page is filtered and paged to, so counting them would say "see their 3 Fighters" to somebody on a tier filter whose builder has thirty. Pluralised, and a payload with no count falls back to the bare wording rather than rendering "see their 0 Fighters". **THE CLOSE CONTROL MUST BE ANCHORED TO THE PANEL, AND PINNED ON A PHONE.** It was position:absolute with nothing positioned between it and #dhcg-veil, so top:12px meant twelve pixels from the top of the VIEWPORT -- and the veil padding reserves env(safe-area-inset-top) while an absolutely positioned child ignores padding. On a notched phone in the installed app the only close control sat under the status bar; the reported symptom was having to tap the very edge of the screen, which is the veil click-to-close, all that was left. #dhcg-panel is position:relative now, and under 760px the control is position:fixed at env(safe-area-inset-top)+10 with a 44px minimum, because the panel is nearly the whole screen there and the panel scrolls INTERNALLY (an absolute child scrolls away with the art). Measured: 390px -> fixed, 65x44, unmoved after scrolling the panel to its end, elementFromPoint returns the button; 1200px -> absolute, anchored to #dhcg-panel. Escape and the veil tap stay as second ways out. ONE COPY, TWO CALLERS: the Collection opens it on a card click, dhcfighters.php opens it the moment a save lands so the Fighter just built can be reviewed, downloaded and posted without going to find it. Host sets `$dhcm_user`, `$dhcm_base`, `$dhcm_deeplink` and then drives `window.DHC_MODAL.open(f)`. `DHCM_RENDERED` guards against being emitted twice, which would duplicate every id in it. **EVERY RULE FOR THE PANEL MUST LIVE IN THE PARTIAL.** `#dhcg-close` was left behind in dhcgallery.php when the panel was extracted -- the lift started at the `#dhcg-veil` rule and that one sat above it -- so the Collection kept its styling while the assembler rendered a bare browser button: white, rounded, system font, in the middle of a dark panel. Nothing warns about a rule only one of two callers has, and neither page is wrong on its own. The harness scans every `<style>` in dhcgallery.php, dhcfighters.php and dhc-assembler.php for selectors naming anything the panel emits. Scoped to the names it ACTUALLY emits, read from its own markup -- matching the bare `dhcg-` prefix flagged the Collection page's own `.dhcg-wrap`, `.dhcg-head` and `.dhcg-grid`, which are nothing to do with it. **ONLY THE COLLECTION OWNS ?fighter=** -- `$dhcm_deeplink` is true there and false on the assembler, where rewriting the address to a different page would send a refresh somewhere else. SAVE NO LONGER RELOADS ON A TIMER: it fetches ajax/dhc-fighter.php and opens the panel; the reload happens on CLOSE (onClose, one-shot), because closing is the natural end of "I made this" and a 900ms timer the player cannot see is a worse place for a page change. Every failure path -- no serial, no panel, bad fetch -- falls back to exactly the old reload. ajax/dhc-fighter.php returns the same object the Collection puts in `data-f`; widening the SAVE endpoint to carry layers, parts, stats and ranks would put presentation inside a write endpoint, and a write that fails because a rank pool could not be read is a Fighter that saved and said it did not. THE ASSEMBLER'S OWN X BUTTON IS GONE: x.com/intent/post shows the image the SHARED URL declares, and an unsaved build on a login-gated page has no URL and no card -- that button could only ever post text. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (sharing)    | Share on X, and the ranks on the Collection modal | dhc-assembler.php (#sharex), dhcgallery.php (#dhcg-share, #dhcg-rank, ?fighter=), dhc-ranks.php, plus dhc-share-harness.js. RANKS, NOT SCORES, ARE THE SHAREABLE CLAIM: "#4 deadliest of 108" is something a stranger can weigh; "6,846" is a number with no scale. The modal shows all four axes from dhcf_rank_axes(); a post names the BEST TWO (four reads as a stat dump and X truncates anyway). Ranked against the WHOLE collection, never the page's current filter -- "#3 of 9 legendary" is a different and much weaker claim than "#3 of 108". dhcf_rank_pool() is read ONCE per page, outside the row loop (it is disk-cached, but re-reading it per row is still a file read and a json_decode each time); the harness counts the call sites. THE TAIL INVITES THE READER AND THEN CREDITS THE ARTIST, the credit in its own PARAGRAPH under the invitation (blank line between them, so it reads as a separate statement rather than a second sentence of the pitch): "Join @skulliance to assemble your own Fighter and battle other players in the Arena!\nArt by @MMAXI404" -- the post is an invitation first, and the credit is a fact it carries rather than its opening line -- none of this exists without Maxingo's art, so a Fighter going out uncredited is the wrong default and the credit is part of the tail rather than something a player has to remember -- a bare handle tells a stranger who made the thing and nothing about why to join, and the next player is the point of the post. It costs ~104 characters, which is why the stat sentence is conditional: the credit and the invitation both outrank the health number. One string, in dhc-fighter-modal.php, now that the panel is the only place a share is offered. **X COUNTS ANY URL AS 23 CHARACTERS** and cuts the overflow off the END, which is where the invitation lives -- a post that loses its handle looks fine to the poster and reaches nobody. Both buttons budget 280 - 24 - handle, the same arithmetic shareOnXUrl() does server-side. The name column caps at 48 so real data cannot reach the limit, which is exactly why the harness drives the truncation path with an oversized name instead of hoping a fixture trips it: a mutation deleting the budget survived the realistic fixture. The harness READS X_HANDLE OUT OF dhc-fighter-modal.php rather than declaring its own, and UN-ESCAPES it: the capture is source text, so a \\n in the literal arrives as two characters where the browser sees one, which both misreads the post and overstates the tail length in the budget -- it had stubbed '@skulliance' while the shipping tail had grown to 84 characters, so every budget assertion was measuring a string 73 characters shorter than the real one. A real post measures 228 of 280 including the URL. SHARE IS OWNER-ONLY in the Collection, the same rule the download follows -- posting somebody else's assembly, prefilled in your voice, is not a share. The assembler's button is the X mark alone (five labels would wrap) and starts **disabled** until the ranks strip answers, because the post is built from those placements; a failed rank fetch disables it again. Not offered in the sandbox, where every trait is unlocked and a rank would be a claim about a build nobody earned. `?fighter=SERIAL` opens that card on arrival (replaceState, not pushState) and is cleared on close, AND makes the page's og/twitter tags about that one Fighter -- its own indexed query, not a scan of the page's rows, or a link to a Fighter outside the current filter would not unfurl. **THE POST CARRIES THE FIGHTER, via dhc-card.php** (see its own row). x.com/intent/post cannot attach a file; the only image a post shows is the one the shared URL declares. NOT A LOOSENING OF dhc-download.php: that still gates the named, full-size, ready-to-post file. The card draws from dhcf_render_fighter(), whose output already lives in dhcrenders/ and is already fetched by Discord's servers for every announcement. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (share card) | The 1200x630 image X renders | dhc-card.php, plus dhc-card-harness.php. **1200x630 AND NOT THE SQUARE RENDER**: summary_large_image is 2:1 and X centre-crops anything else, so handing it the 1000px square beheads the Fighter and cuts its feet off. The art is fitted WHOLE inside the frame (height minus 36px of air) and the sides are filled with a soft, dark wash of the same art, so the card reads as one picture rather than a portrait on a slab. **THE BLUR IS A DOWNSCALE-THEN-STAGED-UPSCALE, NOT IMG_FILTER_GAUSSIAN_BLUR.** That filter is a fixed 3x3 kernel: on a 1200px canvas even several passes leave the art essentially sharp, and the first card shipped with crisp spikes down both sides competing with the Fighter. Resample to DHCC_BLUR (28px), then back up through 120 and 400 with gaussian passes at each -- NOT in one jump, because GD stops interpolating usefully at a 40x ratio and renders hard squares, a mosaic that reads as a rendering fault. Then DHCC_DIM (-110) and a colorize towards the platform ground; at -72 the sides still read as art. Both of those were only visible by rendering a real card and LOOKING at it. The harness measures them now, and measuring the mosaic took three attempts: neighbour-difference does not separate the two cases because the dim clamps most of the backdrop towards black, so what is asserted is the LONGEST RUN OF IDENTICAL PIXELS (86 staged vs 341 one-jump on its fixture, threshold 200). Its fixture is a fine CHECKERBOARD for the same reason -- against the flat red square it used first, both the mosaic and the undimmed-backdrop mutations passed. NO TEXT ON THE IMAGE: there is no TTF in this repo and GD's bitmap fonts at 1200px look like a mistake -- and the post itself already carries the name and placements, which X renders directly above the card. Draws through dhcf_render_fighter(), never its own compositor (the armless-torso swap and single-arm clip are the bug class that cost the Arena its effects twice). SAVED Fighters only, keyed by serial, `disassembled_at IS NULL`; there is deliberately NO ?build= form, which would be a way to walk the trait art out without owning any of it. Cache key is the TRAITS HASH, not the serial -- an edit keeps the serial, so a serial-keyed card would serve the old picture for a week. Atomic rename, nonzero check, `ob_start()` around db.php (display_errors is ON and a notice before the header is a corrupt card that X then caches), and **no imagedestroy()** for the same reason it was removed from dhcfighters-notify.php. The harness LIFTS THE REAL COMPOSITING BLOCK OUT AND RUNS IT against a source whose top and bottom rows are colour-marked: if either marker is missing from the output the art is being cropped. |
| games-dhc-fighters.md (roster)     | DHC Fighters: sorting the roster, and the page's three columns | dhcfighters.php, plus dhcfighters-roster-harness.php. SORTS: newest/oldest/rarest/least-rare/deadliest/toughest/name. "Least rare" is the one that answers the actual question - which Fighter has the most to gain from an edit. POW AND HP COME FROM dhca_build_fighter(), the Arena's own engine, NOT a second formula computed here: a roster that called a Fighter deadly while the Arena disagreed would only be found out after a loss. dhcarena-engine.php is safe to require from this page (no $conn, no $_SESSION, no echo) but it pulls only dhcarena-roles.php - dhcf_rarity() comes from dhcfighters-lib.php, which the page already has and a harness loading the engine alone does not. The sort is CLIENT-SIDE: the cards are all rendered and paged in the browser already, each carrying its own wiring and 250px layer stack, so it reorders nodes with appendChild rather than re-rendering. Every comparator ends in the same two tiebreaks so equal Fighters never shuffle. Sorting resets to page 1 - landing mid-list shows you the cards you were already looking at. Stored in localStorage inside try/catch on BOTH read and write, and the stored key is validated against ORDERS before use. THE ROSTER LIVES IN THE PICKER COLUMN. Embedded, .picker is position:absolute filling the shell's height (canvas + buttons + ranks + the whole draw-order list), and one slot's worth of thumbnails almost never reaches the bottom of that -- on Torso it left most of a screen empty. dhc-assembler.php renders an optional `$dhca_aside` STRING after .grid; dhcfighters.php buffers the roster panel into it with ob_start()/ob_get_clean() BEFORE the include. **BEFORE THE INCLUDE MEANS BEFORE ANYTHING THE ASSEMBLER DEFINES EXISTS.** `$dhc_base` was set at the top of dhc-assembler.php and read by the card loop, so every buffered card shipped with an empty src and `Undefined variable $dhc_base` printed into its alt text. It is `dhcf_art_base()` in dhcfighters-config.php now -- the one file both the assembler and the page already require -- and both call it. (Not to be confused with dhcf_trait_name()'s own scan of the same four candidate directories for trait-index.json; different file, separate loop.)  A string, not an include, because the assembler has to stay something the public dhcsandbox.php can use with no roster, no session and no database -- hence the !empty() guard, and the harness checks the sandbox never sets it. The aside is `flex:0 1 auto; max-height:50%; min-height:0; margin-top:auto`. max-height stops a tall roster pushing the picker past the shell it is positioned inside; **margin-top:auto is what PINS it to the bottom** -- .grid is flex:1 and absorbs the slack, so it ends up there anyway today, but by side effect, and the roster must not move when you click from a 42-thumbnail slot to a 6-thumbnail one. Verified at 60, 6 and 0 thumbnails: top stayed at 590px in all three. SAVE FIGHTER LEADS THE ASSEMBLER rather than trailing it -- below the shell it sat under the draw-order list on one side and twenty roster cards on the other, which is the busiest place on the page for the one control the page exists to reach. CLICKING A CARD SCROLLS TO .shell WITH block:'start', not the canvas with block:'center': the canvas is 66vh, so centring it put its midpoint at the viewport's midpoint, i.e. almost the top of the PAGE, and you scrolled back down to see what you just clicked. `.shell{scroll-margin-top:12px}` keeps it off the edge; the navbar is position:relative so nothing covers it. NO DOWNLOAD ARROW on the roster cards -- right for a 150px card in a full-width panel, twenty arrows of noise at half that size and seven to a row; selecting a Fighter puts Download 1000px PNG on the canvas beside it and dhc-download.php is untouched. Cards shrink to a 96px floor in that column (it is a character select, not the gallery) and PER_PAGE is 20, not 12 -- at seven-plus to a row a dozen was two rows and a pager. **BELOW 900px NOTHING SCROLLS INSIDE ANYTHING.** Desktop keeps its inner scrollers and should: the picker is a real column beside a canvas, the roster is capped at half of it, the all-time ladder fills what monthly leaves. Stacked on a phone none of them has a column height to fill, so the same declarations only produce short windows onto long lists -- scroll the page to the traits, then scroll again inside them, with Fighters past the second row clipped away entirely. Mobile lifts `.picker{max-height}`, `.dhcf-aside{max-height,margin-top}`, `.grid{flex,overflow}` and `.lb-ath{overflow}`. TWO CASCADE TRAPS, both of which defeated a first attempt and both now pinned by the harness: (1) the assembler's `<style>` is emitted AT THE INCLUDE POINT, i.e. AFTER the host page's own, so an equal-specificity mobile override written in dhcfighters.php loses -- the picker and aside rules have to live in dhc-assembler.php; (2) within the assembler, the picker section redeclares `.grid` with its own `overflow:auto` further down the file, so the mobile `.grid` rule must sit AFTER that declaration, not with the other mobile rules higher up. Computed overflow-y at 400px was still "auto" until it was moved. The ladder's breakpoint is 900, not 760, or between the two it is stacked and still scrolling inside itself. Measured at 400/900: zero inner scrollers, zero clipped elements; at 1400: grid auto, aside 50%, ladder auto. THE COLUMNS BELOW SPLIT WHERE THE ASSEMBLER SPLITS. .dhcf-panels tracks `minmax(0,calc(var(--stage) + 2px))`, not an auto-fit 50/50, or the page runs two vertical rules a couple of hundred pixels apart one below the other. `--stage` is declared on `.dhcf-wrap` as well as `.shell` (same value, so .shell is fine inheriting it or declaring its own -- dhcsandbox.php has no .dhcf-wrap). **NO COLUMN GAP, AND THE EDGE THAT MATTERS IS THE RIGHT PANEL'S LEFT ONE.** The ladder's left edge and the picker's left edge are the same column of the page and the eye reads them as one line. A gutter makes alignment impossible -- it puts the right panel 14px past wherever the left one ends, so only ONE of the two edges can meet the picker's border. A first pass aligned the LEFT panel's right edge and it still read as crooked, because the misaligned edge was the one being looked at. .shell has always solved this with `gap:0` and a shared border; the panels row mirrors it: `column-gap:0; row-gap:14px`, first track `calc(var(--stage) + 1px)` (the +1 is .shell's own border -- measured shell 14, stage 15, panel 14), and `.dhcf-games{border-right:0}` so the seam is ONE rule, the ladder's left border, not two touching. Measured: picker and ladder rules both [653,654) at 1200/1400/2000. Below 900px they stack and .dhcf-games gets its right border back, or it renders as an open box. DISASSEMBLE IS A TRASH ICON, not a word: it was the longest label in a three-control row on a ~96px card and rendered as "DISA", and a clipped word reads as a layout fault while saying nothing. Inline SVG, not the emoji (different shape, weight and colour per platform, and it would not take currentColor on hover); `flex:0 0 auto` so it does not take a 1fr share from Edit and Rename; name kept in title + aria-label; the confirm() stays, because an icon carries less warning than the word did. Measured: Edit 23px + Rename 51px + trash 20px in a 111px row, nothing clipped on any card. TWO COLUMNS BELOW, NOT THREE:  all-time and monthly were separate panels and "Where traits drop" was a full-width block beneath them, so a desktop player scrolled past two half-empty ladders to reach the one thing answering "what should I play next". One ladder panel (monthly first - it is the short, live list) and the trait-drop list, in that order: drops before ladder, because the ladder is the only one of them about other people. The harness pins that order, the panel COUNT (two, not three -- and the count pattern must exclude the wrapper's own class="dhcf-panels", which carries the same prefix) and, now, that the two are SIBLINGS. Counting could not catch what shipped: a misplaced `</div>` left .dhcf-ladder nested inside .dhcf-games, so the count was still 2, PHP still linted clean, and the grid had one child -- both panels rendered full width and stacked, which is the exact layout the change existed to replace. The harness walks the divs and checks each panel's parent. The ladder fills its column because grid items stretch: .dhcf-ladder is a flex column, .lb-ath is flex:1 with overflow-y:auto. BOTH it and .body need **min-height:0** - a flex child's default min-height is auto, so without it the table refuses to shrink and overflows the panel instead of scrolling in it. Under 760px the nested scroller is turned off; a scroller inside a page that already scrolls is a trap. MEASURED, not assumed: headless Chrome against the real stylesheet gives 3 columns at 1400px, 2 at 1000, 1 at 400, no sideways scroll at any, and every sort verified by driving the select. Note Chrome's --window-size cannot go below ~500px, so a "400px" test silently lays out at 500 - use Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride AND give the fixture a viewport meta, or it lays out at 980. | DHC Fighters: earn traits, assemble Fighters, compete on rarity | dhcfighters.php (assembler/workshop), dhcgallery.php (the collection browser -- also requires dhcarena-engine.php, PURELY for numbers: dhca_build_fighter() is side-effect free, so health, power, crit and fighting style are derived per row and shown on every card, sortable by Deadliest/Toughest/Hardest hitting and filterable by kit. Rarity score answers what a Fighter is WORTH and these answer what it can DO; the two are near-uncorrelated by design and the gallery used to show only the first -- PUBLIC since it is the shop window, shows owners regardless of profile visibility since Fighters are not holdings), dhc-assembler.php (layering rules, shared with dhcsandbox.php -- both are platform pages now, and $dhca_mode vs $dhca_standalone is what keeps the sandbox's powers separate from its old skin), dhcfighters-config.php (game->category map, tier tables, score floors, DHC2F numbering from 421; DHCF_DAILY_CAP = 3 per source per day, overridable per source via 'cap'). Not every source is a game: maxingo, dailystreak and raids award from db.php directly rather than through the claim endpoint, which is why the cap is enforced inside dhcf_award() rather than only in ajax/dhc-claim-drop.php. DHCF_GAMES['dailystreak'] carries 'unlisted' => true: it stays in the array because everything functional reads it (dhcf_award, the cap, the claim endpoint, the notifier) and removing it would stop the streak paying, but dhcfighters.php skips it when rendering "where traits drop", which is a list of games to go and play. That also leaves exactly 12 rows, which is 2 clean rows of 6 at the repeat(6,...) breakpoint, dhcfighters-lib.php (scoring, consumable inventory, save/disassemble, boards), dhc-dropmodal.php (shared drop modal, DHC_DROP()), ajax/dhc-claim-drop.php (the trust boundary -- floors, daily cap and tier tables live server-side), ajax/dhc-save-fighter.php, ajax/dhc-update-fighter.php (edit in place -- keeps serial/name/created_at, recomputes traits+score+hash; dhcfighters.php?edit=<id> reloads with dhcf_available()'s \$ignore_id so the Fighter's own traits are selectable again), ajax/dhc-rename-fighter.php, ajax/dhc-delete-fighter.php. Tables dhc_trait_drops (append-only ledger; also the source of newest_trait_at, which is why disassembly can hard-delete a Fighter without opening a monthly-board replay) and dhc_fighters (holds LIVE Fighters only -- disassembly deletes the row and frees its serial, and dhcf_next_serial() hands out the lowest unused number from DHCF_SERIAL_START); see dhcfighters-schema.md. Rarity tiers derive from on-chain frequency across the 226 minted DHC2 Fighters (policy b31a34ca2b08bfc905d2b630c9317d148554303fa7f0d605fd651cb5) via dhc/gen-rarity.py -> dhcrarity.php. Layering exceptions and assembly offsets are recorded in dhc/LAYER-MANIFEST.json (gitignored with the art). A TRAIT SET CAN ONLY EXIST ONCE: dhcf_hash_taken()/dhcf_hash_holder() are checked INSIDE the save and update transactions (outside is a race both writers win), and disassembly frees the combination. This REVERSES an earlier documented decision that nobody should ever be blocked -- the old reasoning was that a player holding one background, one torso and one head has exactly one possible character. That risk is real but small: a dressed Fighter is 1 of ~89 billion, the bare minimum build 1 of 36,750, so the refusal message names the holder and says to change any one piece. dhcf_is_first_build() is gone and the FIRST badge with it (gallery, roster, save/edit messages); DHCF_ORIGINALITY_BONUS is deliberately KEPT and now applies to every Fighter, because removing it would make every stored rarity_score stale until dhcf-rescore.php ran over the lot. A SLUG IS ONLY UNIQUE WITHIN A CATEGORY: nine of them are both a head and a torso (dh-alien-punk, golden-cyborg, silver-cyborg, mk100, mk200, c73, dh-cop-2, inferno-wasp-guardian, planet-8tz-specter) and are different traits with different art, different rarity and separate ledger rows. dhcf_committed() and dhcf_shortfall() therefore key on "category|slug", and blockedReason() in dhc-assembler.php compares s.dir (the category) and not the slot key -- weapon/weaponBack and effects1/effects2 share a dir, which is exactly when a second copy SHOULD be counted. Keying by slug alone made the alien punk head spend the alien punk torso. dhcf_category_of() is the same mistake preserved for callers that have nothing but a slug; prefer the category you already have. Tethered weapon halves (scythe/sash, krusher/sash) live in DHCF_TETHERED + dhcf_tethered_partner(); dhcf_award() awards BOTH halves via dhcf_commit_drop(), marking the second with DHCF_PAIRED_MARK in source_detail. THREE queries count the daily cap and all must exclude that mark or they disagree: dhcf_award()'s own check, dhcf_drops_today() (drives the "N of N left today" line), and ajax/dhc-claim-drop.php. The assembler's COUPLE table generates its mutual pairs from DHCF_TETHERED; the axe -> morning star link stays hand-written there because it is one-way UI convenience, not a tether. Head/headgear incompatibilities live in DHCF_HEADGEAR_EXCLUDED_BY_HEAD + dhcf_headgear_blocked() in dhcfighters-config.php and are enforced in FOUR places, all of which must stay in step: dhcf_layers() (every PHP renderer), paint() in dhc-assembler.php (the canvas keeps its own JS copy), blockedReason() (the picker, both directions), and dhcf_save_fighter()/dhcf_update_fighter() (the trust boundary -- a Fighter saved before a rule landed still holds the pairing, so renderers drop the layer rather than assume it cannot occur). FULL-SIZE DOWNLOAD (dhc-download.php, dhc-download-harness.php). dhcf_render_fighter() now takes a $size; the endpoint asks it for 1000, which is the size of every file under dhc/web/1000, and streams the PNG as an attachment. No second composer -- the reason is spelled out at length in dhc-compose.php's header and applies verbatim here. THE 500 KEEPS THE UNSUFFIXED CACHE NAME (f<serial>-<hash>.png; other sizes get '@<size>'): putting the size in every filename orphans every render already in dhcrenders/ and makes Discord, the Arena and the landing thumbnails each recompose a thousand-pixel stack. $size is clamped to 100..1000 -- above 1000 is upscaled blur and the memory to match. MEASURED: 1000px is 673 KB and 124 ms cold, 2 MB peak, then cached forever by traits hash. The only size-dependent arithmetic is DHCF_NUDGE * $size / 1000, and the harness catches a regression there by rendering both sizes and comparing the 1000 scaled down against the native 500 (correct: mean channel difference 0.07-0.68 of 255; nudge hardcoded back to 500: 2.46; threshold 1.5). THE ARM CLIP IS NOT POLICED BY THAT and the harness says so: DHCF_ONE_ARM_SPLIT has a deliberate ~200px tolerance window, so a wrong split still lands on plain torso (0.68 -> 0.75, inside noise). The filename rule lives in dhcf_download_filename() in the LIBRARY, not the endpoint, so it is testable without a web request -- an unnamed Fighter's display name IS its serial, so the naive form gives DHC2F0123-DHC2F0123.png. OWNER ONLY -- and it shipped PUBLIC first, which was wrong. The argument was that the Collection is public and the 1000px layers are already served to anyone who opens a Fighter; a flattened, named, ready-to-post picture of somebody's assembly is still theirs to hand out and not a stranger's to take. Signed-out requests get 403; another player's serial gets 404, NOT 403, because 403 confirms the serial exists. Ownership is the WHERE clause (`AND user_id = %d`), the same idiom as ajax/dhc-rename-fighter.php -- not a read then a compare. TWO FORMS: `?serial=` for a saved Fighter you own, and `?build=<json>` for the arrangement on the assembler canvas, which usually has no saved row at all. The build form is gated on dhcf_owned() -- NOT dhcf_available(), because a trait committed to another saved Fighter is still a trait you own and still something you are entitled to a picture of. THE BUILD FORM ALSO REQUIRES A WHOLE FIGHTER -- dhcf_missing_required(), the same DHCF_REQUIRED trio a save insists on. Without it ?build={"head":"x"} renders that head alone on a transparent 1000px canvas and the endpoint becomes a way to walk Maxingo's art out one clean layer at a time; reported by the user, not caught in review. The ownership check does NOT cover this -- every extraction build uses traits the player genuinely owns. dhc-assembler.php mirrors DHCF_REQUIRED so the button says what is missing instead of navigating into a refusal, exactly as dhcfighters.php's save button does; the server is the authority. The ?serial= form needs no such gate: those traits come from a row dhcf_save_fighter() already validated. An unsaved build is named DHC-Fighter-build-<6 hex of the traits>.png so repeats reuse the filename instead of piling up as '(1)', '(2)'. The endpoint reads $_SESSION with an isset() guard the ordinary pages skip: they can afford a warning, this one is streaming binary and one notice in front of the PNG is a corrupt download -- that exact warning is how the harness caught the missing session_start() in its own stub. PLACEMENT: the assembler bar, next to 'Copy link to this build', because both act on the canvas; $dhca_can_download is a caller-set flag so the SANDBOX does not get one (it unlocks every trait and the endpoint would refuse them). Plus a corner arrow on your own roster cards -- that row (Edit/Rename/Disassemble) already fills a 150px card exactly, measured, and a fourth control pushed it 7px over with Disassemble at min-content, so the arrow is an absolutely-positioned SIBLING of .art (.art is a button and buttons do not nest). NOT in dhcgallery.php; there is a comment there saying why, because it is the obvious place to put one back. THE ENDPOINT IS TESTED OVER REAL HTTP, because what breaks it is not reachable from a function call: the response headers, and whether any stray output got in front of the PNG. db.php runs with display_errors on and prints nothing today -- one echo or one notice turns every download into a corrupt file with no error anywhere -- so dhc-download.php buffers the include and the harness's stub db.php deliberately prints while loading. The DATABASE IS STUBBED BY SHADOWING db.php ON include_path: the endpoint says `include 'db.php'` with no leading dot, so a db.php in the temp dir wins; everything else is the real library, renderer, art and headers. FOUR TRAPS, all hit writing it: (1) shell_exec() BLOCKS on a backgrounded `php -S`, redirects and all -- it reads the pipe until EOF and the server never closes it; use proc_open with the descriptors on /dev/null. (2) $http_response_header is deprecated in 8.5 and prints a notice per request, so the request goes over a raw socket. (3) THE SERVER MUST BE STARTED WITH -d output_buffering=0. With the ini's buffering on, PHP buffers db.php's echo for free and deleting the endpoint's own ob_start() still passed -- measured, a false green. (4) PHP's + on arrays keeps the LEFT value, so `$plain + array('arms' => ...)` silently tested the plain arms; use array_merge. Reverts confirmed to fail it: ob_start() removed (11 checks), Content-Disposition dropped, the 404 guard removed, Content-Length wrong. COMIC COVERS AND DEMOTED COMPANIONS (DHCF_COVER_EFFECTS, DHCF_COMPANION_OVER_COVER; dhc-layerorder-harness.php). DHCF_COMPANION_UNDER moves a companion to just before Arms -- but the slot order is arms, effects1, effects2, head, headgear, so 'below Arms' unavoidably means below Effects too, and the comment only ever claimed the first. A comic cover therefore drew OVER Code Sea Predator while drawing UNDER every ordinary companion: the same frame, two opposite answers depending on which pet the Fighter carried. NO POSITION IN A FLAT STACK IS BELOW ARMS AND ABOVE EFFECTS, so the cover moves down to just before the companion rather than the companion moving up. Only when the cover is currently ABOVE the companion -- an undemoted companion is already last, and moving the cover to meet it would put the frame over the head. Consequence worth knowing: arms now draw over the frame in this pairing (they already did for head, headgear and weapon). DH VISION SHOULDER CAM CAME OFF DHCF_COMPANION_UNDER ENTIRELY, reported the same day: it was demoted because a shoulder mount drawn in front looks stuck to the outside of the character, but below Arms is also below Effects, head and headgear, and headgear drawn over a shoulder cam looks worse than the problem it solved. No stack position is under the arms and over the head, so it went back to the default (last). DHCF_COMPANION_UNDER now holds one entry, which makes two rows of dhc-layerorder-harness.php's matrix coincide -- it says so rather than letting them read as two cases.
