TWO THINGS THE DEGRADED CASE FORCED: a stat falling back to 0 is DROPPED, not shown -- a homepage announcing "0 NFTs staked" argues against itself, and it is the one number a sceptic believes instantly -- and the tile row uses minmax(140px,200px) columns with justify-content:center, because plain 1fr made a single surviving tile stretch the full 860px and look like a bug, which is exactly what a database blip produces. The art strip repeats until a HALF-track exceeds any viewport and keeps an EVEN count, the same -50%-loop trap as the Fighter marquee on dhcgame.php. |
| games-oculus-lounge.md (VIP gate)  | Who may play the Oculus Lounge reskin | HOW TO GET IN IS TWO STEPS AND THE SECOND IS THE ONE PEOPLE MISS: buy the VIP token, THEN register it in the Oculus Lounge Discord. The gate tests a Discord ROLE (966399108011163678), not the token in the wallet, so "buy a VIP token" on its own is incomplete advice -- it already turned away someone who owned one. THREE LINKS, ONE COPY: dropship/vip-links.php holds OCULUS_VIP_BUY (the wayup listing, filtered to the "Legendary - VIP" rarity tier -- the bare collection is the whole Disco Solaris drop), OCULUS_VIP_DISCORD (discord.com/invite/fNuBhYnnuR, the CANONICAL invite, not the t.co shortlink it arrived as) and OCULUS_SITE, plus oculusVipLinks($intro) which renders them as one sentence. Rendered by the Play gate in dashboard.php, the Where to Buy section in instructions.php, and discoin.php, which is read by exactly the person who has no token. skullpaper/games-oculus-lounge.md repeats the three URLs because Markdown cannot include PHP; launchpad-harness.php asserts they still match the constants, that no page has hand-copied one back, and that each page still calls the renderer. That harness's no_comments() strips TRAILING line comments, not just whole-line ones: each require_once carries a "// oculusVipLinks()" note and that note alone satisfied the check for the call, so deleting the call passed. Its line-comment regex uses ~ as the delimiter (a ! delimiter is closed early by the ! in the lookbehind) and skips a slash-slash preceded by : or / so it does not eat https:// in a URL. dropship/dropship.php ($vip), dropship/dashboard.php (the Play gate), dropship/role.php (assignRole), dropship/ajax/transaction.php (DISCOIN pass), process-oauth.php (the roles snapshot), dropship-vip-harness.php. IT IS DISCORD ROLES, NOT THE CHAIN. Owning the VIP token in a connected wallet does nothing here: the Oculus Lounge policy id (3d250a78...) appears in the codebase ONLY inside two wayup.io hyperlinks, and nothing queries Koios or nfts for it. $vip is set from a single role id, 966399108011163678, found in $_SESSION['userData']['roles']; assignRole() confirms that role lives in guild 966397496978964500 (Oculus Lounge), and process-oauth.php does scan that guild. THE ROLES ARRAY IS A LOGIN SNAPSHOT -- process-oauth.php fills it once from four sequential Discord calls and getUsersGuildsRoles() silently ignores a failure, so one rate-limited call drops every role from that guild until the next login, and a role granted after sign-in does not exist to this page. THAT IS THE PAID PATH: buying a temporary pass assigns the real role through the bot AND sets $_SESSION['userData']['VIP'], but the snapshot predates the purchase -- and that flag was written in two places and READ IN NONE, so the gate refused a player who had just paid until they logged out and back in. dropship.php now honours it, as a GRANT ONLY ($vip !== 'true' && !empty(...)), so a missing flag never takes access from someone the snapshot approved. CONFIRMED FROM A REAL ACCOUNT: holder of both the token and the role, refused, and logging out of Skulliance and back in fixed it -- the snapshot, nothing else. So dashboard.php now RECHECKS the role live when the snapshot says no: dropshipMemberHasRole() in dropship/role.php, bot-authed against guild DROPSHIP_GUILD_ID, throttled to once per 300s in the session and run ONLY on the refusal path, so a player the snapshot already approves never costs an API call. IT ONLY EVER GRANTS. The function returns true/false/NULL and null means Discord could not be ASKED, which is not the same as 'no'; even a definite false is ignored by the caller, because an outage must never lock out someone already playing. 404 (not in the guild) is a real false, 429 and an unparseable body are null. Load order matters: dashboard.php must require role.php AFTER webhooks.php, which is where $bot_token comes from. DROPSHIP_DISCORD_API exists so the lookup is testable -- dropship-vip-harness.php points it at a local php -S and drives all six outcomes (holds / does not hold / 404 / 429 / junk / no token), because a function whose whole job is one HTTP call is otherwise only checkable by reading it. No curl_close() in the new function: no effect since PHP 8.0, deprecated from 8.5, display_errors is ON. |
| platform-discord.md (embed limits) | Every Discord post the platform makes | webhooks.php: skl_embed_trim(), SKL_EMBED_*_MAX, the status check in discordmsg(), plus discord-embed-harness.php. THE MONTHLY PAYOUTS RAN ON 2026-10-01 AND WERE REPORTED AS "the monthly script didn't run" -- 208 CARBON credits, 5,086,926 paid, every reward flag closed, and not one announcement in Discord. Two defects, the second of which made the first undiagnosable: (1) OVER A LIMIT, DISCORD REJECTS THE WHOLE POST -- no partial embed, no plain-text fallback. A leaderboard post lists up to 45 ranked players at ~107 chars each = 4,815 against the 4,096 description cap, overflowing from the 39th entry, so a board stops announcing itself the month it passes 38 ranked players. Caps: title 256, description 4096, footer 2048, and 6000 across ALL text fields together (which is why description is budgeted a second time against title+footer+author). skl_embed_trim() cuts on a line boundary when one is within the last 40%, so a rank list ends on a whole entry, and measures with mb_* -- Discord counts CHARACTERS, and a byte-based cut both over-trims emoji and can split one into invalid UTF-8. (2) discordmsg() READ curl's RESPONSE INTO A VARIABLE AND NEVER LOOKED AT IT, so a rotated webhook (401), a deleted one (404), an over-limit embed (400) and a rate limit (429) were all indistinguishable from success -- and run_reward_jobs() swallows stdout on top of that. It now checks for a 2xx RANGE (Discord answers **204 No Content**, so `== 200` is wrong), error_logs the status and body, and calls alertAdmin(). THE ALERT RE-ENTRY GUARD IS LOAD-BEARING: alertAdmin() reports BY CALLING discordmsg(), so without `static $skl_alerting` a dead default webhook alerts down the dead webhook forever. Static, not local -- a local resets every call and guards nothing. Alert signature is channel+status, not the message, or a 12-result board raises 12 alerts. NOTE WHICH CHANNEL: every monthly and weekly leaderboard post passes no $channel, so they all go to the DEFAULT getWebhook() feed -- if that one credential dies, every board goes silent at once while the per-game channels keep working. | webhooks.php (skl_local_image_path + discordmsg), dhc-render-harness.php. BROKEN EMBED IMAGES, intermittent, every channel, for a long time -- Fighters, trait drops AND raids. The origin was ruled out by measurement, not argument: skulliance.io answers Discordbot in 0.27s, serves 60 requests at 12 concurrency with zero failures, does not vary by user agent, has no WAF in front (LiteSpeed, no proxy headers), prunes nothing, and the file behind a failed Fighter embed is a valid 224,773-byte PNG that opens fine. RAIDS ARE WHAT SETTLED IT: they use realm theme art that has existed for months, so no story about when a file was written can explain them. The only thing the three share is that Discord's proxy has to reach this server at post time, and a miss is cached against the URL. So the dependency is gone: discordmsg() resolves any image URL on our own domain to its file and UPLOADS it as multipart (payload_json + files[0]), with the embed pointing at attachment://<name>. Anything third-party, missing, or over 7MB falls back to the URL exactly as before. TRAPS: the multipart branch must NOT set Content-type (curl writes its own boundary; forcing application/json makes Discord reject the post), and PHP folds files[0] into $_FILES['files']['name'][0] rather than one entry per file. Tested by driving the REAL discordmsg at a local php -S standing in for Discord and reading what arrived -- shimming the webhook needs ALL ~24 $webhook assignments replaced, several of which are function_exists(...) ? ... : "" ternaries, and the JSON branch posts a raw body so the probe must read php://input, not $_POST. |
| dist/flexbox.css (dropdowns)       | Every <select> on the platform | dist/flexbox.css, plus analytics.php, merch.php, auctions.php, raffles.php. EVERY DROPDOWN IS SQUARE. The site squared its panels, buttons and cards page by page and left the selects rounded behind them, so on most screens the one round thing left was a dropdown. `select { border-radius: 0 }` in flexbox.css is the baseline for any select with no rule of its own; the ones that DO have a rule are zeroed WHERE THEY ARE DECLARED, because an id or a class beats a bare tag and a page's own <style> comes after flexbox.css regardless. Zeroed: .dropdown, #filterNFTs, #filterRealms, #filterLeaderboard, .wallet-connect select, .trend-select, .merch-select. auctions/raffles bundled input+textarea+select in one rule -- the select is SPLIT OUT rather than overridden after, so the text fields keep their 6px and a sweep sees the truth instead of a 6px rule cancelled three lines later. NOT TOUCHED, and named in the harness so the exclusion is a decision rather than a regex accident: #theme-select-button, #select-boss-button, #theme-select-container, #character-select-container, #boss-select-container, #rg-track, .podium-section (buttons and panels that merely have "select" in the name), and #theme-select, which is a dead rule -- no element with that id exists anywhere. dropdowns-harness.php SWEEPS flexbox.css and all 60 inline <style> blocks rather than checking a list, so a new page cannot quietly add a rounded one. |
| dist/flexbox.css (PWA bottom strip) | Standalone-mode chrome | `body::after` in the `@media (display-mode: standalone)` block paints a fixed strip across the bottom of the VIEWPORT. It exists only to mask the gap under a lifted #quick-menu -- and #quick-menu is on realms.php, missions.php and dropship ONLY, while body::after applied to every page. On everything else in the PWA it was a dead band covering the bottom ~44px of the screen at every scroll position (safe-area inset + 10px). Reported on DHC Fighters hiding the Arena stats on trait claims; rubber-banding exposed them and releasing put them back under. body's padding-bottom did NOT compensate, and cannot: padding extends the DOCUMENT, a viewport-fixed strip covers the SCREEN. Now scoped `body:has(#quick-menu[style*="block"])`, which tracks the inline-style toggle live and has the right polarity -- before the JS runs there is no inline style, so nothing paints. Where :has() is unsupported the selector drops out and the strip disappears, which is the safe direction. Verified across all four states: no menu / menu before JS / menu hidden / menu shown. |
| dropship/dist/flexbox.css (top strip) | Drop Ship + Oculus Lounge burger clearance | `.container::before` under `@media screen and (max-width: 700px)`, checked by dropship-topstrip-harness.php. ONE STYLESHEET, TWO GAMES: Oculus Lounge is dropship project_id 4 and shares this file, so the bug and the fix land on both. #burger-icon is position:fixed and takes no space, `.container{padding-top:60px}` clears it, and this pseudo-element paints that gap (without it the gap shows body's blended wallpaper instead of dark chrome; #0a1929 is the site's own card colour, user-picked off the real page). IT WAS position:fixed, AND THAT IS A PAINT-ORDER BUG, NOT A POSITIONING ONE. A positioned box with z-index:0 paints above every non-positioned in-flow element (CSS 2.1 Appendix E: in-flow backgrounds step 4, inline content step 7, positioned z-index:auto/0 step 8), so the strip rode the viewport and sliced the soldier cards in half the moment the page scrolled. Reported on both games. The gap it exists to paint is at the top of the DOCUMENT, not the viewport, so position:absolute is where it always belonged -- it scrolls away with everything else. Measured in headless Chrome at 393x852 against this exact stylesheet: scrolled 600px, fixed painted over the artwork from y=0 to y=60; absolute leaves it visible from y=0 and still paints the gap at scroll 0. DO NOT reach for z-index:-1 instead -- it fixes the symptom but keeps a viewport-locked band showing through every transparent gap between cards. The harness pins the position, that the strip height still equals the clearance padding (a mismatch renders as a line of wallpaper under the burger), that it still paints a colour, that #burger-menu (99) and .navbar (1) still outrank it, and that no OTHER rule in the mobile block is a top-anchored opaque fixed bar at z-index <= 0. It collects EVERY max-width:700px block, not the first -- there are two, and slicing the wrong one makes every check fail against the nav rules. |
| dhc-render-harness.php (the stand-in) | A harness that poisoned its own later runs | It spins up `php -S` on a FIXED port 8791 and cleans up at the bottom of the file -- which a fatal, an exception or a Ctrl-C all skip. One fatal (discordmsg() gained a call to skl_embed_trim() and the harness lifted only discordmsg(), so every run died on an undefined function) left that server listening, still serving the PREVIOUS run's temp directory. Every run afterwards connected to the orphan, wrote got.txt somewhere else and reported three confident failures about webhooks.php that had nothing to do with webhooks.php. TWO FIXES, BOTH CHEAP: a nonce file fetched over the port before anything is trusted, so a stale server is named as a stale server instead of producing phantom failures; and a register_shutdown_function() that terminates the server and clears the directory however the run ends. THE LESSON GENERALISES: a harness that lifts one function out of a real file inherits that function's call graph. Lift its callees too, or the first time somebody adds a call the harness goes red and stays red -- and nothing it prints before the fatal looks wrong. |
| admin-{projects,collections,missions,blockchains}.php | Onboarding a project without the database or FTP | THREE PAGES, not one tabbed panel -- each is a different job (onboard a project once, add collections rarely, write missions for an hour), with their own Admin dropdown in the nav. admin.php stays as a 302 to admin-projects.php for old bookmarks. Shared: admin-lib.php (every rule plus the reads, uploads and admin_chrome(), no output and no db.php), admin-css.php, admin-harness.php. THE LAYOUT BUG THAT MADE THE FIRST VERSION LOOK BROKEN, and the reason the harness now greps for it: header.php leaves a `<div class="container">` OPEN for the page to fill. The tabbed version opened a SECOND container inside it and then a `<div class="column">` -- and .column DOES NOT EXIST in dist/flexbox.css, which only has .col1of2 and .col1of3 (flex: 33%). A classless div in a flex row inside a nested height:100% container is why selecting a project appeared to do nothing: the form submitted, the page came back, and the content had no box to live in. Verified in a browser that the picker's JS was fine before looking anywhere else. Pages now open `<div class="row">` plus a full-width .adm child, and the picker is a real submit button rather than an onchange, so it cannot fail silently without JS. USER 1 ONLY -- deliberately narrower than storeItemEditRights(), which hands a partner creator their own shop listings; this panel creates projects and rewrites mission economics. 404 rather than 403, because an admin panel should not confirm it exists. RIGHTS ARE CHECKED TWICE: once to draw the page, once inside the POST block. A page that gates at the top and then trusts itself is one early return away from an unguarded write, and the harness counts the calls. REWARD AND DURATION ARE DERIVED, NEVER POSTED. The form displays them and does not send them, so a hand-edited field cannot reach the table -- that is what makes the two live typos this work started from (The Worm's duration, Trash Collection's reward) unsaveable rather than merely corrected. The harness greps for $_POST['reward'] and $_POST['duration'] by name. THE NEXT MISSION CONTINUES THE PROJECT'S OWN LADDER rather than imposing a house style: flat-100 projects get 100 x level, wide-spread projects get the next rung of the master sequence, a new project starts flat. See missions-economy.md. TWO PAIRS MUST NOT DRIFT and the harness asserts both against the real missions-lib.php: admin_mission_slug vs mission_art_slug (the art FILENAME) and admin_art_ext vs mission_art_ext (which file is the still). If they disagree the panel previews one name and the browser asks for another, with no error anywhere. IMAGE SIZE IS DECIDED FROM THE HEADER BEFORE ANY DECODE, and this one took the staking site down on 2026-10-05. A 5000x5000 mission upload against a flat 256MB Imagick limit: PAST ITS MEMORY LIMIT IMAGEMAGICK DOES NOT FAIL, it spills the pixel cache to DISK and carries on, turning a two-second resize into minutes. The request never returned, every navigation behind it passed the service worker's 20s NAV_TIMEOUT_MS, and the PWA showed offline.html -- the server was never down and answered in 0.2s from outside the whole time. THE ARITHMETIC THAT MATTERS: ImageMagick allocates Q16, EIGHT bytes a pixel not four, so 25 megapixels is ~200MB for the source cache alone and ~400MB with a working copy. admin_image_budget() now reads getimagesize() (header only, no decode), refuses anything over ADMIN_IMAGE_MAX_MP (30) instantly with the number and what to do, and otherwise sizes RESOURCETYPE_MEMORY to pixels x 16 capped at ADMIN_IMAGE_MAX_MEM (576MB) so the work stays in RAM. A jpeg:size hint lets a huge JPEG decode pre-scaled; PNG has no equivalent. The harness asserts the grant against pixels x 12 and not x 8 -- 8 is the cache alone, and a flat 256MB passes that bar, which is precisely the configuration that hung. UPLOADS TAKE THEIR TYPE FROM THE FILE via finfo, never from the browser-supplied name -- this writes into a directory the server serves. Images go through admin_write_image(), the same Imagick path lib/image-cache-lib.php runs; ajax/cache-nft-image.php already drives that over HTTP, which is what proves the web user can write images into the repo at all. An mp4 is stored as uploaded because there is no ffmpeg and no exec() on this host, and the save is REFUSED unless its paired .gif is there too. mov is not an accepted kind at all. CURRENCY IS A FILENAME: icons/{lowercase(currency)}.png, uniqueness checked case-insensitively because Skull Swap's board dedupes on LOWER(currency) -- two projects differing only in case share one icon AND appear as a single tile. The icon is saved as the currency, never as the uploaded filename. MARKETPLACE: THE LINK IS PER CHAIN, THE IDENTIFIER IS PER COLLECTION, and tangling them is the natural mistake. blockchains.marketplace_url (new column, migration in multichain-schema.md section 1b) holds the printf template -- wayup, xrp.cafe, tensor -- beside explorer_nft, which is the same kind of thing and was already a column; the split was accidental and meant switching marketplace was a DEPLOY. collections.marketplace_slug stays where it is: xrp.cafe addresses a collection by an artist-chosen slug and bootlegs / moneyhorse / vipasana / random-digi-hell-scenes are four LIVE values on four collections of the SAME chain, so it can never be one chain setting. collectionMarketUrl() prefers the slug ONLY ON THE CHAINS WHOSE MARKETPLACE ACCEPTS ONE -- xrp.cafe and Tensor -- and falls back to the on-chain id, validated per chain. WAYUP ADDRESSES BY POLICY AND NOTHING ELSE, so a slug on a Cardano collection would be a dead link; the pre-refactor code had this right by accident because each chain had its own branch and the Cardano one never read the slug, and flattening them into one template made it apply everywhere. Latent rather than broken -- all 263 live Cardano links are 56-hex policies -- but it would have sat there until somebody filled the field in. DO NOT DROP collections.marketplace_slug: of the five live XRPL collections, FOUR have one (bootlegs, random-digi-hell-scenes, moneyhorse, vipasana) and the fifth already falls back to xrp.cafe/profile/<issuer>. Dropping the column downgrades those four from the collection page to the artist's profile, and the slugs are unrecoverable -- there is no lookup between issuer:taxon and the slug in either direction, which is why twenty-one had to be hunted by hand once already. SAFE TO SKIP THE MIGRATION: the three templates are built-in defaults and the column is only preferred when present and containing %s, so every link is byte-identical before and after -- verified. The column is read with str_replace not sprintf, because a stray % in a database column is a ValueError and that is a broken PAGE rather than a broken link. admin-blockchains.php refuses a template with no %s (every collection would link to one page) and disables that field with an explanation if the ALTER has not been run. REORDERING A LADDER IS SAFE BECAUSE THE RUNGS BELONG TO THE PROJECT, NOT THE MISSION. admin_reorder_plan() keeps the project's existing sorted costs as rungs 1..n and only changes which mission sits on which -- so the same costs exist before and after, the project pays the same total over the same days, and all that moves is which story is at which tier. A mission does NOT carry its cost with it; that mutation is tested. Level 1 is the free intro wherever it lands. The posted order must be an exact PERMUTATION of the project's mission ids -- short, long, duplicated or foreign lists are refused outright with no partial write. Two routes, one rule: drag posts the whole order, the up/down buttons post a single move that admin_reorder_move() turns into the same list, and both end in the planner. The buttons are real submits so the ladder is reorderable with the script absent. The UPDATE carries AND project_id so a stray id cannot renumber another project's ladder, and the mission-save block stands down on a reorder post. The index on (project_id, level) is NOT unique, so levels shuffle in place with no two-phase dance. A NEW PROJECT DEFAULTS ITS discord_id TO THE ADMIN (ADMIN_DEFAULT_DISCORD). projects.discord_id is what storeItemEditRights() matches a signed-in creator against, so a blank one means NOBODY can edit that project's store listings -- which only surfaces the first time one needs fixing. The default applies to NEW projects only, written as `$P ? $P['discord_id'] : DEFAULT` rather than `??` on the column: an existing blank was somebody's decision, and substituting on an edit would silently reassign those listings. EDITING A MISSION MOVES ITS ART RATHER THAN ASKING FOR IT AGAIN. The filename IS the title, so a retitle used to leave the old files behind and point the row at names that did not exist -- a broken tile plus one permanent orphan in images/missions/ per rename. admin_art_plan() is a PURE function (no filesystem) that decides renames, deletes and what is still missing; admin-harness.php drives every shape of edit through it, which is the only honest way to test something that deletes artwork. Uploads always win over a rename. A format change cleans up what it replaces -- png to mp4 drops the stale .png, mp4 to png drops BOTH the .mp4 and its .gif. ORDER IS THE SAFETY AND THE HARNESS PINS THE ORDER, not just the calls: uploads, then plan, then RENAME (reversible, rolled back if one fails), then the row, then DELETE last. Deleting before the write is how an edit that fails validation takes the art with it, and a refused write rolls the renames back so files are never left matching a title that was never saved. A dest-filter guarding 'a renamed file is then deleted' was REMOVED as unreachable -- a rename destination is always a required extension under the new slug, and the delete sources are old-slug files or non-required ones, so the sets cannot intersect. A mutation proved it changed nothing. The invariant is asserted in the harness instead, so a future third delete source fails loudly rather than being papered over. MISSION ART FILENAMES ARE CHECKED PLATFORM-WIDE, not per project: the filename is the title, so a collision silently overwrites another project's image.  THE EDIT FORM SHOWS THE CURRENT ART, because rewriting a description means describing a picture and the picture was in another tab. Through mission_art_url() rather than a hand-built path: that is the function the GAME renders through, so the preview cannot point somewhere the player's browser does not, and it carries the ?v=<mtime> without which a just-replaced image shows the old one for a week -- which is the exact state this panel is used to fix. Gated on $Q: on a new mission there is nothing to show and an empty frame is worse than none. An mp4 renders BOTH files, since it is two uploads and either can be the broken one, with the .gif as the video's poster (preload=metadata paints a black box otherwise, and a preview you have to press is not a reference). Missing art is reported through admin_art_missing() rather than left as a broken icon -- that is the case the preview is most useful for. HARNESS NOTE: the CSS assertion must pin the SIZED rule (max-width in px), not merely the selector, because the narrow-screen block repeats '.adm-art img,.adm-art video{max-width:100%}' and a looser check passes with the main rule renamed away. It did, first time. ||
| missions-economy.md *(root)* | Mission cost/reward spread, and mission art | missions-economy.md + missions-tiers.py (the generator), against quests and missions-lib.php. Operator material, so it sits at the root beside multichain.md -- a player never reads it. THE WHOLE ECONOMY IS THREE LINES, recovered from a 455-row dump rather than from memory: level 1 is free (cost 0 / reward 10 / duration 1); above that duration = cost/100 and reward = cost * (1 + level/10). They collapse to ONE INVARIANT -- net per day = 10 * level -- because the cost cancels. So THE COST LADDER DOES NOT SET THE RATE, only how much CARBON is locked and for how long, and cost and duration must move together or that mission silently becomes the best or worst deal on the platform. missions-lib.php already computes net_per_day and shows it to the player, so it is the number a mistake shows up in. ROUND NUMBERS ARE STRUCTURAL: cost is a multiple of 100 and the multiplier moves in TENTHS, so reward is always a multiple of ten. A multiplier on a twentieth breaks it (500 * 1.25 = 625) -- for a finer gradient add levels, never finer multipliers. 453 of 455 rows obeyed it; the two that did not (ids 197, 183) were data-entry slips, found by asserting the invariant over the whole table rather than by reading rows, and fixed live 2026-10-04. THE ONE DESIGN FAULT: the multiplier keys off ABSOLUTE level, so a 46-mission project tops out at 460/day and a 6-mission one at 60/day -- eight times the rate for no reason a player can see. The obvious fix (multiplier from POSITION in the ladder) is the WRONG ONE here: it rewrites every existing row when a project is extended, 26 of 26 cases measured. append_safe() instead makes every field a function of LEVEL ALONE (cost 100*level, duration = level, multiplier capped at 2.0), so appending is a pure INSERT -- 0 of 36 cases rewrote anything. A first attempt capped the multiplier but still churned, because the COST ladder switched families past twelve missions; both have to be level-only or neither is. Retrofit cost is in section 5c and is NOT a decision that document makes. MISSION ART IS NAMED AFTER THE TITLE (mission_art_slug: apostrophes dropped, spaces to hyphens, lowercased), so TWO MISSIONS WITH THE SAME TITLE SILENTLY OVERWRITE EACH OTHER'S FILE, across projects, and any punctuation other than an apostrophe lands in the filename. Zero collisions and zero unsafe slugs in 455 rows -- discipline, not enforcement. AN MP4 MISSION NEEDS TWO FILES, the .mp4 and a .gif beside it for the still; mov is unsupported anywhere in the code, which is why it must be converted before upload. Two bugs fixed here: mission_active() SELECTed q.extension and then hardcoded '.png', breaking the field-list image for 266 of 455 missions (58%), and the share payload handed Discord a .mp4 as an embed image. Both now go through mission_art_ext(), and missions-harness.php guards the rule by COUNTING -- it legitimately appears once, inside the helper, and the call-site count must exclude the declaration or deleting a call site still passes. NO ffmpeg AND NO exec() anywhere, and Imagick has no video delegates, so mp4 -> gif CANNOT be automated on this host; an uploader should refuse an mp4 without its paired gif rather than write a row that renders broken. Resizing/optimising CAN reuse lib/image-cache-lib.php, which already runs Imagick with a 256MB cap, coalesces animated gifs and Lanczos-resizes to 1000px. |
| games-realms.md (the map) | Which realms appear on the faction map | getFactionsRealmsMapData() in db.php, REALM_MAP_ACTIVE_DAYS (30), checks at the end of realms-harness.php. THE MAP NOW SHOWS ONLY REALMS THAT HAVE RAIDED OR DEFENDED IN THE WINDOW. It used to show every realms.active = 1 row ever created, most of which had not moved in months -- mostly dead ground, and the dead ones were also the UGLY ones: user_image is built by concatenating users.avatar into a Discord CDN url, so anybody who changed their avatar and never came back renders a 404 and falls through to the skull icon. Filtering on activity clears most of those as a side effect, because a player who still raids has a current avatar. RAIDED **OR** DEFENDED, both directions -- checking only offense_id would quietly hide every realm that plays defensively, which is the opposite of the intent. FACTIONS NEED NO FILTER OF THEIR OWN: map.js builds its faction list by reducing over these rows, so a faction with no surviving realm never appears. No JS change was needed. DISPLAY ONLY, AND THAT IS THE LINE NOT TO CROSS. Raid targeting is getRealms() in db.php, rendered by realms-attack.php -- a different function on a different page -- so a hidden realm is still raidable and reappears on the map the moment it is attacked. The harness asserts getRealms() does NOT reference REALM_MAP_ACTIVE_DAYS, because the day it does, hiding a realm also makes it unraidable and a cosmetic change has become a gameplay one. A QUIET MONTH MUST NOT EMPTY THE MAP, which reads as broken rather than as quiet: if the filtered query returns nothing the unfiltered one runs and a console.warn says why. The harness asserts the fallback is RUN rather than merely declared -- an unused $all_sql still satisfies a check for its name, which is how that assertion first passed with the fallback deleted. |
| (no page) | The nightly job is reachable from ten pages | verify.php, verify-entrypoint-harness.php. Every job block in verify.php was gated on isset($_GET['verify']) and NOTHING else -- no auth, no CLI check -- and the last one ends in platform-wide payouts. verify.php is included by store.php, realms.php, wallets.php, my-nfts.php, auctions.php, cryptcrawl.php, cryptconquest.php, dhcgallery.php, raffles-verify.php and wallet-ajax.php, so `store.php?verify=1` ran the entire nightly job for anyone who typed it. That contradicts a rule stated elsewhere in this file: the leaderboard snapshot cron is kept read-only and its dispatch deliberately separate because coupling them 'would put payout paths one edit away from an unattended cron'. TWO GATES NOW, and the split is the point. (1) THE ENTRY-POINT CHECK ships ENABLED because it cannot break a cron that already works: realpath(SCRIPT_FILENAME) === realpath(__FILE__) is true exactly when verify.php is what was requested, which covers CLI runs and a URL cron on verify.php?verify=1 unchanged, and nothing else. Both sides must RESOLVE -- realpath() returns false for a non-file and false === false is true, which would hand a pass to php -r. (2) VERIFY_JOB_TOKEN is OPT-IN because turning it on needs a crontab edit and a half-applied change there means nobody gets paid: define it in credentials/db_credentials.php and HTTP requires ?token=<it>, compared with hash_equals; leave it undefined and behaviour is exactly as before. CLI never needs it. |
| platform-collections.md | Collections: chain column + chain filter | collections.php, getPoliciesListing() and chainBadge() and getChainsWithCollections() in db.php, filterPolicies() in skulliance.php, filterChain() in skulliance.js, lib/chain-icons.php, collections-harness.php. BOTH ARE CONDITIONAL ON THERE BEING A CHOICE: with one chain the column is a stack of identical logos and the dropdown is a control whose every option returns the same rows, so neither renders -- count(getChainsWithCollections()) > 1 gates both, and that function INNER JOINs collections so a configured-but-empty blockchains row is never offered. EVERY COLLECTION IS LISTED, STAKED OR NOT. INNER JOIN nfts hid any collection with nothing staked -- no rows, gone from its own registry -- and those are the interesting ones for a staker shopping by reward rate. nfts and users are LEFT JOINed now, WHICH MOVES THE USER CONDITION INTO THE users JOIN: a LEFT JOIN followed by a WHERE on the right table's column is an INNER JOIN with extra steps, since unmatched rows come back NULL and the WHERE discards them. An earlier comment here warned the old shape was 'a trap the moment anything becomes a LEFT JOIN'; that moment arrived the same day. COUNT(users.id), NOT COUNT(nfts.id) -- with the join outer, an NFT owned by user 0 or a deleted user yields a row with nfts.id set and users.id NULL, so counting nfts.id would change every existing total. The project and chain filters stay in WHERE because they are conditions on COLLECTIONS, the left table. The harness asserts both directions, and note it now asserts the OPPOSITE of what it did before the LEFT JOIN -- the rule genuinely inverted. Both filter values are cast with (int) and the harness asserts an injection attempt does not reach the SQL. THE TWO FILTERS COMPOSE: the chain select posts its own form carrying the current filterby in a hidden field and vice versa, so picking a chain does not throw away the project you were looking at; $filterchain is remembered in the session beside $filterby. THE SLUG IS NOT THE FILENAME -- blockchains.slug is 'xrpl', the logo is icons/xrp.png -- and that map is lib/chain-icons.php, SHARED with the homepage band rather than copied, because homepage.php cannot include db.php (WordPress; see homepage-data.php's header) and a second copy of a four-entry map is how four different IPFS gateway lists happened. chain_icon_file() strips path characters out of the slug before it becomes a filename. LOGO ONLY, NAME IN A TOOLTIP: four logos down a column read as shapes, and repeating the word beside each doubles the column to restate what the picture said. THE alt TEXT STOPPED BEING OPTIONAL WHEN THE NAME WENT AWAY -- while the name sat beside it the image was decorative and alt='' was correct; now the image IS the information, so an empty alt leaves the column blank to a screen reader, and title is no substitute (not reliably announced, never on touch). The lettermark fallback carries title + role=img + aria-label too, or a missing icon is the state that loses the information. Names are escaped ENT_QUOTES because they land inside SINGLE-quoted attributes and an apostrophe would close one. THE CHAIN SELECT NAMES ITS OWN AXIS: it reads "Chain" at rest, not "All", because the project select beside it reads "Project" -- its first option IS the label -- and "Project | All" does not say all of what. So nothing is pre-selected at 0. It is styled by #filterChainSel in dist/flexbox.css, matching #filterNFTs; the harness COMPARES the two rules' declarations rather than checking the selector exists, because the narrow-screen rule uses the same selector and renaming the main rule passed that weaker check. Every badge carries an onerror lettermark because icons ship by FTP, so a chain added without one shows CAR/XRP/SOL/POL rather than a broken-image glyph in every row. 17 mutations, all caught -- two of them only after fixing anchors that had landed on the wrong occurrence (the project select also has an <option value="0">All</option>). |
| (no page) | Homepage chain band | homepage.php (#chains section + .hp-chains CSS), hp_chains() in homepage-data.php. COUNTED FROM THE DATABASE, NOT LISTED, and that is the reason it exists in this form: the homepage named its chains in FOUR hand-written strings and when Solana shipped only three got updated -- one still read 'built on Cardano with the XRP Ledger alongside it' months later. A hand-maintained logo band would have been the fifth place to go stale and the most visible. hp_chains() INNER JOINs collections, so a blockchains row that is configured but empty is not advertised (same rule as hp_dhc_fighters(): a marketing section with empty frames is worse than one section shorter), and orders by collection count so Cardano leads because it genuinely does rather than because it was typed first. It returns null rather than '[]' on an empty result so hp_cached() keeps the last good answer instead of caching an empty band for five minutes. THE SLUG IS NOT THE FILENAME: blockchains.slug is 'xrpl' and the logo is icons/xrp.png, named after the asset not the ledger, so $hp_icon_file maps it -- caught by rendering the band, where it showed as a broken image. Mapped rather than renaming the file, which the wallet modal also references and which ships by FTP outside the repo. Icons are the same white-on-transparent marks the wallet modal uses, which is why they work on this dark ground; the onerror lettermark is not decoration, since a host that has not had the icons FTP'd would otherwise print broken-image glyphs on the front page. VERIFYING IT: homepage.php is a FRAGMENT included by the WordPress theme, so a standalone preview has no box-sizing reset and no outer container and its widths mean nothing. Also headless Chrome CLAMPS --window-size to a 500px minimum viewport, so phone-width screenshots taken that way are not phone width -- render the fragment inside an iframe of the width you actually want. |
| platform-wallets.md | Wallets, all four chains | The page was CARDANO-ONLY until 2026-10-06 -- it never mentioned XRPL or Solana, two chains late. It now covers the chain picker, Cardano's stake-pool requirement being Cardano's alone, the optional Polygon network switch, per-chain explorers, and immediate-on-link vs nightly re-read. IT ALSO ANSWERS THE PERMISSION QUESTION, which holders do ask: no wallet offers a read-only connect (MetaMask's docs: eth_accounts also grants eth_sendTransaction, personal_sign, eth_signTypedData_v4), so the prompt is the wallet being honest rather than Skulliance asking -- and the platform hands the permission straight back once the address is recorded. Audited: the only provider methods in first-party code anywhere are eth_requestAccounts and wallet_switchEthereumChain. |
| (no page) | ONE IPFS gateway list | lib/ipfs-gateways.php. There were FOUR (db.php, lib/image-cache-lib.php, verify-solana.php, verify-polygon.php) and they had all drifted. Measured from the server with a CID pinned everywhere AND one only some hosts carry, so 'refusing us' can be told from 'has not got it': four of the six in use answer 429, pinata 301/0, and the only host serving Danketsu's art (ipfs.filebase.io) was in no list at all. image-cache-lib RACES, so being down to one live runner cost no wall clock and looked like nothing. SEPARATELY, IPFS_FALLBACK_GATEWAY in db.php is what the BROWSER fetches for an uncached image and never touches that raced list -- it has now moved twice (ipfs.io -> pinata -> filebase) and the harness asserts it equals the head of ipfs_gateways(), because drift there is a broken image on a row that staked perfectly. Re-measure with `php polygon-probe.php step=gateways`. |
| multichain.md *(root, not Skull Paper)* | Staking on more than one chain | THE DESIGN DOC LIVES AT THE REPO ROOT, not in skullpaper/ -- it is build-and-operate material (migrations, go-live order, node behaviour), not something a player reads, and the XRPL work set that precedent. multichain.md is the reasoning; multichain-schema.md and solana-schema.md are the migrations, both run BY HAND. CHAIN IS A PROPERTY OF A COLLECTION AND OF AN ADDRESS, NEVER OF A PROJECT OR A PERSON (§3d) -- blockchain_id sits on collections, wallets and nfts, all DEFAULT 1, so every pre-existing row was correct the instant the column landed. Put it on projects and you split an artist in two and every project-level feature grows a special case for ever. Nothing in game code may ever test the chain. THE LOAD-BEARING INVARIANT IS removeUsers() BEING CHAIN-SCOPED (§5a): it zeroes nfts.user_id for everyone about to be re-verified, so a pass that cleared another chain's rows would never restore them -- every holder on that chain silently loses their staking, and nothing errors. Same for getNFTAssetIDs() and getAllAddresses(). EVERY CHAIN'S PASS RUNS INSIDE THE ONE NIGHTLY JOB, never as its own cron (§5c): the payouts at the end of that job are platform-wide, so a separate cron races them and a payout landing mid-pass reads a half-rebuilt ownership table and underpays, in money. Isolation comes from each *_nightly() never throwing and carrying its own wall-clock budget, not from a separate schedule. AND EVERY PASS READS BEFORE IT WRITES (§5d): if any address fails, nothing is cleared and nothing is written. Cardano can clear-then-rebuild because nothing interrupts it; the others can be interrupted, and a half-rebuilt table is not a smaller truth, it is a wrong one. |
| multichain.md §13 + solana-schema.md | Solana (chain 3), for OMEN | verify-solana.php, verify-solana-{harness,probe,doctor}.php, solana-wallet-harness.js, ajax/solana-link.php, header.php (chain tile + wallet grid + solanaConnect), verify.php (verify=solana, sol_nightly in the main job). db.php needed NO changes -- every accessor the XRPL work scoped already takes a chain id. BUILT, NOT LIVE: the blockchains row, the collection INSERT and the rate are still to be run by hand. METAPLEX CORE ONLY (CoREENxT6...), not Token Metadata and not compressed NFTs -- a Core asset is ONE account holding owner, collection, name and uri, so ownership is a memcmp at byte offset 1 and one getProgramAccounts call returns a whole wallet with no paging. The name is on-chain too, so unlike XRPL an NFT can never be named after its serial because a gateway failed. Compressed NFTs are not accounts at all and cannot be read this way at any price; the probe says so by name. nfts.ipfs NOW HOLDS EITHER A BARE CID OR A WHOLE URL, which is the one change that reached Cardano. OMEN hosts its art at omenati.com, which is the case §6b parked. THREE readers and only three: processNFT() stores an absolute URL whole instead of substr(7); getIPFS() returns it as-is instead of hanging it off a gateway; lib/image-cache-lib.php gets _ipfs_is_url(), one empty gateway so gateway . value is the URL, and the <46-char CID floor SKIPPED -- that floor would otherwise have dropped every OMEN image before any network activity, since the URL is 33 characters, leaving only a [SKIP] line calling a working URL a malformed CID. The branch is additive: an absolute URL was being mangled before. WALLETS ARE MATCHED ON THEIR OWN NAMESPACE, never on window.solana, which several wallets claim and the last to load wins -- matching on it lets a Solflare click connect Phantom and link the wrong address with nothing in any log. No extensions exist on mobile; the grid says so. Signature verification IS possible here (ed25519 only, lib/sodium_compat already vendored) and is a later additive change, unlike XRPL where the secp256k1 half has no pure-PHP verifier. THE RATE IS THE ONLY NUMBER THAT CAN COST MONEY: 7,209 live assets earn it nightly from the moment the row exists. Double-staking is prevented by the migration being 1-for-1, EXCEPT that the Omen-controlled wallet holding the surrendered Cardano pieces must never be linked as a staker. |
| multichain.md §14 | Polygon (chain 4), for Danketsu | verify-polygon.php, verify-polygon-harness.php, polygon-probe.php, ajax/polygon-link.php, polygon-wallet-harness.js, header.php (the Polygon tile and evmProvider/polygonConnect), db.php (POLYGON_CHAIN_ID, the three link builders). THIS CHAIN READS BACKWARDS. ERC-721 has no "what does this wallet hold" and Danketsu does not implement ERC721Enumerable (0x780e9d63 -- measured, polygon-probe.php prints it), so the COLLECTION's owner map is read instead: ownerOf(1..totalSupply) batched through Multicall3 at 250 per eth_call, intersected against linked wallets. 4,444 tokens = 18 calls, ~180ms each, no API key -- cheap enough for connect time as well as nightly, hence no cache; if one is ever needed it goes in poly_owner_map() and nowhere else. allowFailure is TRUE on every batched call because ownerOf REVERTS for a burned token and this collection has exactly one (4,443 of 4,444 owned); with it false that single id fails the whole batch of 250. PHP CANNOT COMPUTE KECCAK256 -- hash('sha3-256') is FIPS-202, different padding -- so the four selectors are written out in poly_sel() and each was proved against the live contract. Same gap means addresses are shape-validated and LOWER-CASED rather than EIP-55 checksummed: ownerOf answers lower case, MetaMask reports mixed, and if the two are not folded NO wallet matches ANY token and the pass reports a clean zero with nothing erroring. ajax/polygon-link.php folds before STORING, not just before comparing. A GATEWAY URL IS NOT A WEBSITE: Danketsu's metadata points `image` at https://<cid>.ipfs.nftstorage.link/... which 302s then 429s, so poly_gateway_cid() lifts the CID back out and folds it to ipfs://CID/path -- the opposite of what OMEN needs, where an absolute URL on the project's own domain must be stored whole. poly_storable_image() KEEPS the ipfs:// prefix because processNFT() does substr($image, 7); returning the bare CID (which is what the column ends up holding, so it looks right) costs the CID seven characters silently. The harness pins this by lifting processNFT()'s real branch out of verify.php -- it could not be caught by a stub, which is exactly how it got written. ONE WALLET TILE, not three: every EVM wallet shares window.ethereum so there is nothing to choose between, unlike Solana where matching on window.solana can connect the wrong one. The Polygon network switch ASKS and does not require -- eth_requestAccounts returns the same address on every chain and ownership is read server-side, so a declined switch MUST still link. IMAGE SIZE IS A REAL CONSTRAINT HERE: Danketsu's art is 5000x5000 = 25MP = ~200MB resident in Imagick Q16, and image-cache.php runs up to 16 workers; Imagick spills to DISK past its 256MB limit rather than failing, so the symptom is minutes per image and OOM-killed workers, not an error. Use --workers=4 for a bulk run on this chain. Cardano's art never came close, which is why 16 was fine for years. |
| header.php Play menu + DHCF_GAMES   | Member links go to GAMES | A signed-in member's links go STRAIGHT TO THE GAME, never to a *game.php marketing landing. Five in header.php's Play menu (guardians, cryptcrawl, cryptconquest, monstrocity via match3rpg, skullracer) and the same five in DHCF_GAMES['url'] pointed at the pitch, which is an extra click on EVERY attempt to play, forever, to re-read something you accepted by signing in. It was also inconsistent: launchpad.php has always linked straight to the games, so the same Play intent behaved differently depending on which menu you used. SAFE BECAUSE THE PLAY MENU IS MEMBERS-ONLY -- the whole navbar sits inside `if(isset($name))` -- and the public pages keep their audience: homepage.php, site-header.php on every public page, and sitemap.xml. Do not re-point these at *game.php; if a member needs to learn a game, the game's own page should teach them. |
| ui-selectors.php + my-nfts + store  | Platform redesign, pages 1-2 | THE SAFETY NET FIRST. `php ui-selectors.php --snapshot <page> > before.json`, edit, then `--check before.json`: it reports any id or class the page stopped emitting that JavaScript anywhere in the repo still reaches for, INCLUDING the ~140 built by concatenation (getElementById('p' + n + '-image')), which a grep cannot see. Diff-based against the page's own before-state, so there are no false positives from the 771 global selectors. It is static -- rendering needs the database -- so it proves a hook still EXISTS, not that the page still works; drive the page for that. Verified in both directions: silent on an unreferenced id, FAIL+exit 1 on a referenced one.

MY-NFTS.PHP IS THE FIRST PAGE MIGRATED. The pattern for the other fourteen: (1) snapshot, (2) add a data-returning sibling in db.php rather than editing the renderer -- getNFTsData() has the same filters, columns and ORDER BY and NO output, while getNFTs() is untouched because showcase.php and diamond-skulls.php still call it, (3) the page owns its markup and a scoped `<style>`, (4) --check, (5) keep every JS hook. Result here: 9 ids and 17 classes kept, 0 removed, 13 added; db.php +49 lines, 0 removed. THE PLATFORM CARD RULES ARE OVERRIDDEN, NOT EDITED: .nft carries `width:25%; float:left` and .nft-data `text-align:right` -- a fixed four-column float grid from before there was a phone audience -- and both are overridden in the PAGE's inline CSS, so showcase.php renders the identical markup exactly as it did. Do not 'fix' them in flexbox.css until every caller has migrated. NEGATIVE-OFFSET HACKS ARE THE FIRST THING TO CHECK ON EVERY PAGE: flexbox.css claws back space with `position:relative; top:-Npx` in at least eight places -- #filter-nfts is -35px and #filtered-content is -40px, both tuned to close the gap under a bare one-line <h2>. Give a page a taller masthead and they stop closing a gap and start landing content ON TOP of something; here the filter covered the stat tiles and the grid covered the context line. Grep `top: -` before changing any page header. AND SAY REPEATED FACTS ONCE: the rate is per COLLECTION, so a filtered view was printing the same rate, project and collection on all 24 cards -- four of five lines identical, which is what made the grid read as a database report. Fields uniform across the page are hoisted to one context line under the title and dropped from the cards, leaving art and a name. SQUARE CORNERS: DHC Fighters and the Arena have none, and they are the pages that read as modern; the 2.5rem on .nft and 10px on .nft-data are the old platform's. New work is hard-edged -- and the radius is not all in one place: .nft is 2.5rem, .nft-data 10px and .content 10px, so squaring a page means overriding the CONTAINER too, not just the card. ALIGNMENT IS INHERITED, NOT SET: .main carries `text-align:center` for the whole column, so a new element that does not state its own alignment floats centred over a left-aligned h2. State it. NOTHING MAY CROP THE ART: .nft-image is `min/max-height:170px; overflow-y:hidden` with `.nft img { max-height:165px }` -- a fixed window that hard-clips any piece taller than it, which was survivable while the image was a thumbnail and is not once the art leads the card. my-nfts.php replaces it with a 1/1 box and object-fit:contain: uniform grid at 1,483 items, and a tall or very wide piece is letterboxed and shown WHOLE rather than cropped. Verified against 1:1, 1.59:1, 2.06:1 and a 1500x1674 tall piece in one grid.

STORE.PHP IS PAGE TWO, same pattern: getItemsData() added (identical filter, query and ORDER BY, plus the payment options, per-option balance, affordability and already-purchased flags -- that arithmetic is business logic and stays in db.php; only markup moved). getItems() is untouched and still has its one caller, so store.php reverts in a line. renderBuyButton() is still called FROM the page: it emits a form that posts a purchase and must not be duplicated into a template. Checked what the page inherits BEFORE building, which caught four of the five traps for free. THE FIFTH WAS SPECIFICITY: `.nft span { display:block }` is 0-1-1 and beats a bare `.st-meta` at 0-1-0, so new spans inside a .nft rendered as blocks and ran their text together ("Sinder Skullz2 left"). Prefix new card classes with #filtered-content. The store card also now says what a viewer can AFFORD -- a greyed button with the balance beside it, and a masthead count -- which the old page made you read every card to work out. |
| games-skull-swap.md (cascade) | A match left sitting on the board | cascadeTiles() in skullswap.php, checked by skullswap-cascade-harness.js. REPORTED FROM A REAL ROUND: "cleared a bunch of diamond bombs and the cascade left a match of 3 just sitting there". THE RE-CHECK WAS GATED ON `moved || matchCheckCount < 2` AND THAT COUNTER IS A GRACE ALLOWANCE, NOT A BRAKE -- it means "nothing moved, but look twice more anyway", a cheap net for a match that settles a beat late. Nothing stops the cascade chain because nothing RESTARTS it: the chain only continues when resolveMatches() finds a match and the handler that clears it calls cascadeTiles() again, so a check that finds nothing ends there regardless of the counter. Read it as a brake (easy to do) and the fix looks dangerous; read it as grace and the bug is obvious. THE BUG: every cascade incremented it, including productive ones, and only a check that found NOTHING reset it. A bomb chain runs a cascade per blast, so by the time the board settled the grace was spent -- and the one check that mattered never ran. The else branch reset the counter, scheduled nothing, and abandoned whatever was on the board. FIX: ask the board (`const pending = moved ? true : this.checkMatches().hasMatches`) instead of guessing from a counter, and let anything productive reset the grace so it is available when actually needed. checkMatches() only READS the board and is called only when nothing moved -- the one case where it changes the answer. REPRODUCIBLE ON DEMAND, which is what the harness pins: a full board that cannot fall, one three on it, matchCheckCount at 2. Before the fix it resolves at counts 0 and 1 and is abandoned at 2 and above. HARNESS TRAP, hit immediately: asserting "no match exists afterwards" is flaky in BOTH directions, because refills are random and a fresh three can appear while the loop is still legitimately working. The planted match uses an icon that exists nowhere else, and its absence is the assertion. AND WHAT THE TESTS CANNOT SEE: removing the counter's reset, or the counter from the condition entirely, changes nothing observable once `pending` is in front of them -- both were checked by hand rather than left to look covered. The idle-board test is still worth keeping: it is what would catch somebody making the scheduled callback re-enter cascadeTiles(), which WOULD spin. |
| site-header.php *(shared)*          | Public site navigation | site-header.php is the ONE public nav, included by homepage.php, by every public game landing page, and by skullpaper.php FOR ANONYMOUS READERS ONLY -- header.php's entire navbar sits inside `if(isset($name))`, so a stranger arriving at the Skull Paper from search got an EMPTY nav and no way back to anything; a member already has the platform navbar and two stacked bars would be worse than the bug. That page also needs `#skullpaper .sp-side { top: 62px }` when the public menu renders, because its contents sidebar is sticky at top:10px and would scroll under a sticky 51px bar -- the same class of collision as match3rpg.php's exit button. Checked: no id, class or function collisions with header.php or the platform CSS, and .container has no overflow, so sticky survives inside it. Included by (dhcgame, match3rpg, cryptcrawlgame, cryptconquestgame, skullracergame, guardiansgame). It used to be markup+CSS inside homepage.php alone, which is why a visitor landing on a game page had no way to reach the rest of the site. SELF-CONTAINED (own CSS under an `sh-` prefix) because those pages share no stylesheet -- each carries its own inline CSS. NO session, NO db.php: it is navigation, and homepage.php is included by WordPress. ROOT-RELATIVE LINKS, NEVER ABSOLUTE -- cookies here are host-only, so an absolute www. link in SHARED nav would log out everyone browsing the bare domain on every page it appears on; the homepage's old nav had absolute URLs and this move fixes them. STICKY, NOT FIXED: fixed is out of the layout, so every including page would need its own top padding, wrong the moment the bar wraps on a phone. That is why homepage.php's .hp-hero padding-top dropped 120->56 and 96->40 (it was reserving height for the old fixed bar) and why match3rpg.php's #m3-exit moved to top 62px -- it is z-index 99990 against the nav's 9990, so at 8px it covered the skull mark. Two optional knobs set BEFORE the include: $sh_on_home (keeps section links as in-page anchors) and $sh_links (the homepage passes its full 9; the default is the short set a game page wants). skullswap.php, cryptcrawl.php and cryptconquest.php are NOT in the list -- they include db.php and already wear the platform header. |
| platform.md (PWA stalls)           | Installed-app loading failures | service-worker.js, offline.html, header.php (#nav-loader), plus pwa-offline-harness.js. THE INSTALLED APP IS display:standalone, SO A DEAD NAVIGATION HAS NO WAY OUT -- no address bar, no reload, no back. Reported as "always closing the app and reopening it". Three things compounded: (1) **#nav-loader covered the whole screen on every internal link click and nothing ever took it down** -- its bar animated 12s then sat at 90% forever, with no cancel, retry or explanation. That was the biggest of the three and is pure client-side. It now shows a stall panel after STALL_MS (9s: under ~5s it fires on merely slow pages, over ~15s people have already force-quit), naming the likely cause from navigator.onLine / connection.effectiveType / downlink, with **Try again** and **Stay on this page** -- the page underneath never went anywhere. Going offline mid-wait short-circuits the timer (measured: panel in ~120ms instead of 9s). bfcache restore clears the timer or the panel can appear over a page that already loaded. (2) **the service worker was a no-op**, so a failed navigation gave a blank frame. It now intercepts navigations only and falls back to offline.html. **Scope is deliberately narrow**: GET only (a form POST can pay a reward -- never replay one), `mode === 'navigate'` only (sub-resources, fetch and ajax/ untouched), same-origin only; anything else gets no respondWith at all, i.e. the old behaviour. NAV_TIMEOUT_MS is 20s and is a FLOOR, not a deadline -- cutting off a slow-but-progressing page would make a bad connection worse. With no cached fallback it re-fetches rather than inventing a response. (3) offline.html is **entirely self-contained** -- no stylesheet, script, font or image, because it is shown exactly when fetching anything is what does not work. Inline SVG logo. The harness asserts that. STILL NOT CACHED, DELIBERATELY: every HTML response is per-player and live (balances, timers, raid state), and flexbox.css / the game JS are unversioned URLs, so a cached copy would survive a deploy while version.php's update banner told people to refresh into the old stylesheet. If asset caching is ever added it must be invalidated by that VERSION signal. pwa-offline-harness.js loads the real worker in a vm sandbox with faked caches/fetch and drives every branch; the loader was also driven in headless Chrome (normal load never shows the panel; stalled at 9.7s; cancel restores the page). |
| platform.md (Launchpad section)     | Post-login landing     | launchpad.php ($lp_sections registry = every tile; process-oauth.php:~198 redirects here, NOT profile.php; header.php top-level link). Nav-only + 3 cheap stats (getWallets/getCurrentDailyRewardStreak/getCurrentBalance) -- NO writes, NO game logic, because it is the first page after login and must never be what breaks. Start Here strip is conditional on those 3 stats and self-clears. Emoji icons, not images (FTP deploy). The staked-NFT view is my-nfts.php (was dashboard.php -- renamed because the name promised an overview it never gave). dashboard.php REMAINS as a permanent 301 stub and must not be deleted: 46 references plus bookmarks, Discord links and in-game "back to Skulliance" buttons still point at it. Its page identifier for filterNFTs()/renderVisibility() is now "my-nfts", and skulliance.php accepts BOTH that and the old "dashboard" for the #holdings anchor. dropship/dashboard.php is a DIFFERENT file and is untouched. TWO PATH CHECKS keyed off the old filename and broke silently in the rename -- skulliance.php gating verifyMembershipNFTs() on REQUEST_URI, and skulliance.js gating the NFT upload button on window.location.pathname. Both now accept my-nfts.php (and still the old name). Anything else that keys off a page NAME rather than a link will fail the same quiet way. Game exit buttons (match3rpg, skullswap x2, monstrocity, displayRound() in skulliance.js, and dropship/header.php) go to launchpad.php, not the NFT view -- Drop Ship was the last one still exiting to dashboard.php, from before it moved into this repo, and it is the only in-repo "back to Skulliance" button that ever pointed there; wallet-ajax.php still redirects to the NFT view after connecting a wallet, which is deliberate. |
| marketplace-store.md *(new)*        | Free member claims     | store.php, ajax/item-edit.php, store-edit-harness.php (EDIT AFTER SUBMISSION. Two editors: user 1 on every listing and every field, and a PARTNER CREATOR on the listings credited to a project they own. OWNERSHIP IS BY PROJECT because there is nothing else -- `items` records no submitter, so the only link between a listing and a person is projects.discord_id, the same link renderItemSubmissionForm() uses to decide who may submit at all; a creator can own several projects, so it is a set. db.php storeItemEditRights() answers that ONCE and both the page and the endpoint ask it; a page that decides for itself which buttons to draw drifts from the endpoint that decides which writes to accept, and the endpoint is the one that matters. The endpoint checks BOTH ENDS of a move -- the stored project_id and the posted one -- so owning a listing today does not let anyone push it onto another artist's shelf. `featured` and `secondary_project_id` are admin-only and are OMITTED from a partner's UPDATE rather than rejected: reading `$_POST['featured'] ?? 0` would clear an Exclusive flag every time its owner fixed a typo. store-edit-harness.php pins all of that down by running the real endpoint in a subprocess against a fake connection -- verified to FAIL when the admin-field gate is removed. NOTE `include '../db.php'` resolves against the WORKING DIRECTORY (a path starting with a dot bypasses include_path), which is why it works under mod_php and why the harness runs with cwd=ajax/. The gate is in the endpoint, checked against the session; store.php's $st_admin/$st_mine only decide whether a button is drawn, and a button nobody can see is not a permission. db.php adminUpdateItem() whitelists the columns it will SET and intersects that list with SHOW COLUMNS FROM items -- not information_schema, which the cPanel user gets #1044 on -- because `secondary_project_id` and `featured` are read unprefixed out of an items+projects join and nothing in the repo proves which side owns them; an UPDATE naming a projects column would fail the whole statement. The modal reads its values off the card's own data-* attributes, so opening it costs no round trip, and it posts to ajax/item-edit.php rather than to store.php -- skulliance.php:404 creates an item on any POSTed `name`, so a form falling back to a normal submit would duplicate the listing it was meant to fix. Save reloads rather than repainting the card: price, currency options, affordability and the featured-first ORDER BY are all server-side. db.php's older updateItem() is dead code covering four of eight columns; left alone rather than widened. quantity 0 delists (the store query is `WHERE quantity != 0`), -1 is unlimited. |
| marketplace-auctions.md *(new)*     | Bid-based NFT sales     | auctions.php, db.php:9379-9577 |
| marketplace-raffles.md *(new)*      | Ticketed raffles        | raffles.php, db.php:9602-9828 |
| platform-activity.md *(new)*        | Platform activity feed  | activity.php, activity-lib.php, activity-schema.md, the write inside discordmsg() in webhooks.php, activity_prune() from the nightly block in verify.php, nav link in header.php, plus activity-harness.php (120 assertions, every one verified to FAIL under mutation). **IT IS A LOG, NOT A QUERY, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE DESIGN.** The obvious build merges the feature tables by date; it cannot work, because a large share of what Skull Bot announces is ASSEMBLED AT ANNOUNCE TIME AND STORED NOWHERE -- a monthly standing, a raid summary, a trait roll written out in words exist as a sentence in an embed and as nothing else. So the row is written where the sentence already exists, inside discordmsg(), which covers all 100+ call sites at once and puts a new feature in the feed the day it ships. The cost is that HISTORY STARTS EMPTY and no faithful backfill exists. **THE WRITE IS INSIDE THE 2xx BRANCH**, not next to the json_encode(): a post rejected for a rotated webhook (401), a deleted one (404), an over-length embed (400) or rate limiting (429) reached NOBODY, and a feed whose premise is "this was announced" must not carry it. An unconfigured channel never gets there at all -- the enclosing `if ($webhook != "")` already skipped it. **alertAdmin() IS EXCLUDED, BY TWO FLAGS.** Those are ops failure reports with an @ping down the DEFAULT webhook, and the page is readable by every member. `$skl_alerting` (static, so it is still true inside the nested call) covers the alert raised from discordmsg()'s own failure branch; `$GLOBALS['skl_ops_alert']`, set by alertAdmin() in a try/finally, covers alertAdmin() called from anywhere else. The finally matters: a leaked flag silently kills the whole feed for the rest of the request. **TWO ACTOR IDS.** $actor_id defaults to null = resolve from `$_SESSION['userData']['user_id']`, which is right for nearly every announce because the player's own request is what triggers it (cron correctly records 0; an announce made about player X from an admin's request mis-attributes, which costs one row in one filter). $actor_id2 CANNOT be inferred and must be passed -- dhca_announce() is the platform's only two-sided announce and passes ($attId, $defId). THREE SCOPES, and the third is not the second: `mine` is user_id only, `involving` is user_id OR user_id2. An Arena defender never made a request, so collapsing them would file losses they did not initiate under their own activity. **NESTED ANCHORS BROKE THE CARD AND IT IS NOT HYPOTHETICAL.** activity_format() turns a Discord `[text](url)` into an <a>, and the card was itself an <a>; the parser closes the outer one early, so the card's whole body was hoisted OUT of its link. Caught in the browser (one card's .ac-link had a single child where every other had three), not by reading. The card is now a div with an empty `.ac-stretch` overlay (position:absolute; inset:0; z-index:1) and `.ac-desc a` at z-index 2, so in-text links still go where they say. **text-align needs declaring on .ac-link, not just .ac-card**: flexbox.css has a bare `a { text-align: center }` and an element selector beats a value inherited from the card, so the first fix corrected only the unlinked cards. activity_format() is the XSS BOUNDARY for the page -- htmlspecialchars() runs FIRST and the markdown is applied to the escaped string, so the only tags in the output are the ones it builds; link hrefs are restricted to http(s) and re-escaped, and card hrefs are checked the same way. TRUNCATION IS mb_-AWARE because titles are full of emoji and a half-cut character is invalid UTF-8 that costs the whole row on utf8mb4; it is done in PHP rather than left to MySQL because STRICT MODE MAKES AN OVER-LENGTH VALUE AN ERROR, NOT A TRUNCATION. A MISSING TABLE IS THE EXPECTED PRE-DEPLOY STATE, not a failure: mysqli_report is OFF so query() returns false, every call is @-suppressed (display_errors is ON and this runs inside the request carrying a player's own result back to them), the page renders an empty wall, and the nightly prune reports "no table yet" rather than alerting. Retention ACTIVITY_RETAIN_DAYS = 120. **NOT FILTERED BY users.visibility**, on the owner's explicit call ("if it goes to discord I think it's safe to mirror to activity") -- but note platform-dashboard.md tells players that setting controls whether "activity appears publicly", so the docs and the behaviour disagree. user_id/user_id2 are stored, so honouring it is one WHERE clause in activity_recent(), no migration. `general`, `member`, `elite`, `innercircle` are dead channels and will simply never appear. **MENTIONS ARE RESOLVED, NOT FLATTENED.** activity_format() first replaced every `<@id>` with the words "a member", which on a live Gauntlets card produced "a member was defeated by a member (mato_b13)" -- two different players erased from a sentence whose entire content was which of them won. activity_mention_names() now looks up `users.discord_id -> username` for a WHOLE PAGE in one query (a leaderboard or Gauntlets run posts a dozen announcements naming the same few people; per-card lookups would be dozens of round trips for one answer), and it is called AFTER the +1 row is popped so the undrawn card pulls no names. "a member" survives as the fallback for an id with no account here. The username is player-supplied and is substituted by a callback running AFTER htmlspecialchars(), so it is escaped separately -- that is a hole if anyone inlines it. **THE CHANNEL SELECT MUST DECLARE ITS OWN COLOURS**: the rule set only padding and font-size at first, leaving everything to the user agent, so on a phone it was the one white rounded system box on a dark page. Values are flexbox.css's own dropdown convention (#filterLeaderboard / .dropdown): #0d1e2e ground, #D6DDDE text, square. `#ac-filters select option` is styled SEPARATELY because a select's background does not inherit into its popup list on Windows or Android. The segmented control is square for the same reason and its padding is 8px so both controls come out 33px. |
| platform-dashboard.md *(new)*       | Staking portfolio       | dashboard.php |
| platform-gallery.md *(new)*         | NFT discovery           | gallery.php |
| platform-collections.md *(new)*     | Policy registry         | collections.php |
| platform-leaderboards.md *(new)*    | All leaderboards        | leaderboards.php, db.php:4194-5637. AVG LEVELS ARE FORMATTED: the all-time Monstrocity board ranks on AVG(level) and MySQL returns an AVG as a DECIMAL, so the hub card read "Lvl 28.0000" and the row under "Avg Level" matched it; the monthly board uses MAX(level), an integer, which is why only one of the two ever looked wrong. lbAvgLevel() rounds to one decimal and drops a trailing '.0' (28.0000 -> 28, 27.6667 -> 27.7). number_format() runs FIRST so the string always contains a '.', which is what stops the rtrim pair turning "20" into "2" -- the harness pins that case explicitly. Used by both the hub card and the board row so they cannot disagree. WHY THE SWITCHER WAS INVISIBLE ON DESKTOP AND FINE ON A PHONE: flexbox.css pulls #filtered-content up 40px and #filter-nfts (Find a Project) a further 35 and right-aligns it -- rules shared with store/my-nfts/showcase/collections, so they cannot be changed there. The far right of that row was therefore ALREADY occupied, and margin-left:auto put the toggle underneath the project <select>. Measured live: toggle at x1543 y154-178, #filter-nfts y136-166, elementFromPoint at the toggle's centre returned the select. On a phone the media query stacks them, so it showed. Fixed by scoping `#filtered-content{top:0}` and `#filter-nfts{top:0}` to this page, dropping margin-left:auto so the toggle sits next to the back link, and giving .lb-subhead position:relative+z-index:1. Content now begins 14px below the subhead (its own margin) instead of 26px above its bottom edge. BOARD PICKER: renderLeaderboardPicker() puts a grouped <select> of all 23 boards at the right of the same row, so changing BOARD no longer needs the hub either. It targets leaderboardShortestPeriod() -- Weekly, else Monthly, else All-Time -- because the switcher is adjacent, which keeps it one entry per board. No hub entry: the back link is that. THE TWO DROPDOWNS ARE ONE PAIR: filterLeaderboard() used to emit #filter-nfts inside the results panel with its own label style and flexbox.css's #filterLeaderboard (190px wide, 30px tall, 20px bold, 8px radius) -- next to a board picker that looked nothing like it -- and it rendered a BLANK box on a board page because no option matched. It emits .lb-pick markup now, both sit in a .lb-picks wrapper at the right of the subhead, and one rule styles both. That rule must name `.lb-pick select#filterLeaderboard`: a bare `.lb-pick select` (0-1-1) loses to flexbox.css's id (1-0-0). Defaults: the selected option is the current project when $filterby is numeric, otherwise a "Choose..." placeholder -- never blank and never appearing to claim whichever project sorts first. The Leaderboard Hub option is gone (the back link in the same row is the better affordance), and #filterLeaderboardForm needs display:none or the hidden form is a zero-size flex ITEM that still collects the row's 18px gap. Labels are "Games:" and "Projects:" (was "Find a Project:"). filterLeaderboard() is called ONLY from leaderboards.php, so none of this is shared. PERIOD SWITCHER ON A BOARD PAGE: opening All-Time and then wanting Monthly meant going back to the hub and finding the card again -- two navigations to change one word, and no way to compare periods. renderLeaderboardPeriods($filterby) prints the sibling periods next to the back link, driven by leaderboardBoardFor(), which reverse-looks-up the slug in $SKULLIANCE_BOARDS -- THE SAME 'periods' map the hub cards are built from, so the two cannot drift: add a period to the catalogue and both grow it. 23 boards, 20 of them multi-period. A one-period board renders NOTHING (on the hub the row is always rendered so card heights match; on a board page a lone inert "All-Time" is a label pretending to be a control). Slugs are compared AS STRINGS because one of them is numeric -- '15' is the delegations board -- and $filterby arrives as a string from the query and as an int elsewhere; that cast, not PHP-7 loose-compare folklore, is what the harness pins (since PHP 8, 0 == 'activity-ath' is already false). leaderboards-harness.php evals the catalogue and both functions straight out of db.php and checks every period slug round-trips to its own board. |
| platform-analytics.md *(new)*       | Public transparency dashboard | analytics.php, ajax/analytics-content.php, ajax/analytics-trends.php, plus analytics-metrics-harness.php. ANALYTICS HAD FALLEN A YEAR BEHIND: the cards covered three games and the trend chart offered 14 metrics, while the platform had shipped eleven games. Now 23 trend metrics in three <optgroup>s (Platform / Realms & Missions / Games) and a card for every game, plus a Specialty Games row for Drop Ship and Oculus Lounge. ONE DEFINITION OF A PLAY, THREE PLACES. db.php's Activity `$sources` (~line 7730) decides what counts: completed delves only for cryptcrawls/cryptconquests (`status IN ('won','lost')`), resolved encounters for Gauntlets (`outcome != 'pending'`), one row per fallen realm for guardians_scores, SUM(attempts) rather than COUNT(*) for the two match-3s, runs not solves for Obscura (`active = 0`). ajax/analytics-trends.php and ajax/analytics-content.php mirror it exactly; change one, change all three. DATE COLUMNS ARE NOT UNIFORM AND NOT GUESSABLE: skull_racer_runs `created_at`, gauntlets_encounters `resolved_date`, dhc_arena_battles `started_at`, dhc_fighters `created_at`, dhc_trait_drops `awarded_at`, nfts/realms/missions/raids `created_date`, everything else `date_created`. Each is taken from that table's own CREATE in the repo (db.php for the racer, obscura-lib.php, guardians-lib.php, dhcarena-schema.md, dhcfighters-schema.md) or from the Activity board's per-source `$w_*` filters. Guessing wrong gives an EMPTY CHART, not an error. `$metrics` entries are now a raw FROM clause, not a bare table name (Gauntlets needs the `ge` alias), so the backticks came off -- safe because `$metric` is checked against the map's keys before any of it reaches a query. A FAILED QUERY NO LONGER LOOKS LIKE AN UNPLAYED GAME: the endpoint returns an `error` key and logs the SQL, and the page prints a message over the canvas instead of drawing an empty chart. NULL periods are dropped (history from before a table got its date column, per db.php's migration note). DROP SHIP AND OCULUS LOUNGE HAVE NO TREND, DELIBERATELY: own database via dropShipDbConnection(), and `results` has NO date column at all -- a run is filed against a game ROUND. Their cards show This Round / All Time instead, and the section is skipped entirely if that database is unreachable rather than rendering a row of zeros. UNDATED ROWS ARE A THIRD STATE, AND THE ONE THAT BIT: `guardians_scores` has 39 sieges live and a NULL `date_created` on every one of them, so All Time drew an empty chart that read as a game nobody plays. guardians-lib.php documents the column as `DATETIME NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` and guardiansFinishRun()'s INSERT does not name it, so the live column does not match the doc -- it was added without the default. THE SAME NULL BREAKS THE MONTHLY REALM GUARDIANS LEADERBOARD, which is the exact failure db.php's migration note warns about: all-time looks fine while the dated views quietly report zero. FIX: `ALTER TABLE guardians_scores MODIFY COLUMN date_created DATETIME NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP;` -- new sieges stamp, the 39 historical rows stay NULL because there is no honest source for when they happened. Until then the endpoint counts the NULL bucket into an `undated` key instead of dropping it, and the page says "39 recorded, none of them dated" and prints an under-chart note when a partial gap would silently undercount a line. Only All Time can see these: every other range filters `DATE(col) >= ...`, which NULL fails. TWO-CARD ROWS USE .ana-row-2: the DHC pair and the Specialty pair sat in a 3-column grid at two-thirds width with a hole where the third card would be. analytics-metrics-harness.php cross-checks the <option> list, metricLabels, `$metrics`, `$SKULLIANCE_BOARDS` (so a new game cannot ship without a trend -- exemptions are an explicit list with reasons) and the Activity `$sources` fragments, and verifies each date column against its CREATE where the repo has one. |
| platform-profile.md *(new)*         | Profile + streak cal    | profile.php  GAMES PANEL: profile-lib.php profile_game_record(), plus profile-harness.php. The page predates nine of the platform's games and showed none of them. One panel, not nine sections -- each game has its own page and leaderboard already.
